Building upon the isotropic local law, we determine the singular value locations of
\widetilde{A}
in Theorem
29
and achieve the control of
\left\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\left(G(z)-\Phi(z)\right)\mathcal{U}\right\|
as given in Lemma
28
. These instruments have been previously explored in the previous work by O’Rourke, Vu and the author
[
64
]
. In this paper, we refine these estimations and ease the conditions in our earlier work
[
64
]
. Furthermore, we deploy these refined tools to derive a variety of new perturbation bounds.

To explain the idea of deriving perturbation bounds, we simply focus on the largest eigenvector
\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
of
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
. In practice, it is necessary to consider both
\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{r+1}
, as they jointly involve the largest singular vectors
u_{1}
and
v_{1}
. Nonetheless, for the sake of clarity, we will momentarily disregard the terms related to
{\mathbf{u}}_{r+1}
and
\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{r+1}
to illustrate the main ideas.

We start with the decomposition

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}=({\mathbf{u}}_{1}{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{\mathrm{T}})\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}+\mathcal{P}_{1}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}+\mathcal{Q}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1},

(42)

where
\mathcal{P}_{1}=\mathcal{U}_{1}\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}
and
\mathcal{U}_{1}
is the matrix of eigenvectors of
\mathcal{A}
excluding
{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
. Meanwhile,
\mathcal{Q}
is the orthogonal projection matrix onto the null space of
\mathcal{A}
. The challenge in establishing perturbation bounds for
\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
lies in quantifying the latter two terms on the right-hand side of (
42
).

First, for the
\ell_{2}
analysis, we aim to bound
\sin\angle({\mathbf{u}}_{1},\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1})
. By taking the Frobenius norm on both sides of (
42
), we obtain

1=\cos^{2}\angle({\mathbf{u}}_{1},\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1})+\|\mathcal{P}_{1}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|^{2}+\|\mathcal{Q}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|^{2}.

Rearranging the terms yields

\sin^{2}\angle({\mathbf{u}}_{1},\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1})=\|\mathcal{P}_{1}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|^{2}+\|\mathcal{Q}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|^{2}.

A straightforward linear algebra argument allows us to bound
\|\mathcal{Q}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|
by the noise-to-signal ratio
\|E\|/\sigma_{1}
. The main task is then to establish a bound for
\|\mathcal{P}_{1}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|\leq\|\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|
, which effectively comes down to bounding
|{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}|
for
j\neq 1
. We explain how to achieve this bound below.

From the equation
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}=(\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{E})\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{1}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
, we can express
\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
as

\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}=(\widetilde{\lambda}_{1}I-\mathcal{E})^{-1}\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}=G(\widetilde{\lambda}_{1})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}

and further rewrite it as

\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}=\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{1})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}+\left(G(\widetilde{\lambda}_{1})-\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{1})\right)\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}.

Hence, for
j\neq 1
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}={\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{1})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}+{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\left(G(\widetilde{\lambda}_{1})-\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{1})\right)\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}

(43)

Calculations similar to those in (
34
) indicate that the first term on the right-hand side of (
43
),
{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{1})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
, is exactly
\lambda_{j}\alpha(\widetilde{\lambda}_{1}){\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}
(omitting the term containing
{\mathbf{u}}_{r+1}
). We continue from (
43
) to get

\left(1-\lambda_{j}\alpha(\widetilde{\lambda}_{1})\right){\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\approx{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\left(G(\widetilde{\lambda}_{1})-\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{1})\right)\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}.

To control
|{\mathbf{u}}_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}|
, we apply Theorem
29
to analyze the coefficient
1-\lambda_{j}\alpha(\widetilde{\lambda}_{1})
that precedes it. Lemma
28
is applied to manage the term on the right-hand side.

Next, for the
\ell_{\infty}
analysis, from (
42
), we obtain

\|\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}-({\mathbf{u}}_{1}{\mathbf{u}}_{1}^{\mathrm{T}})\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\mathcal{P}_{1}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{\infty}+\|\mathcal{Q}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{\infty}\leq\|\mathcal{U}\|_{2,\infty}\|\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|+\|\mathcal{Q}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{\infty}.

The bound for
\|\mathcal{U}_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|
has already been established in the preceding
\ell_{2}
analysis. The second term,
\|\mathcal{Q}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}\|_{\infty}
, can be bounded by considering the fact

\mathcal{Q}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}=\mathcal{Q}\left(G(\widetilde{\lambda}_{1})-\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{1})\right)\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{1}

and then applying Lemma
28
.

These are the main ideas that we have incorporated in our proofs. Before concluding this section, we would like to highlight that the results presented in this paper can be extended to scenarios where the noise matrix
E
contains independent sub-Gaussian entries. This extension would rely on a lemma similar to Lemma
27
, which can be demonstrated using the tools provided by random matrix theory. However, due to the technical complexities involved, we have chosen to reserve the discussion of this extension to sub-Gaussian cases for a forthcoming paper.
It remains a highly interesting direction to further establish these perturbation bounds when the noise matrix
E
comprises heteroskedastic random variables. We believe that new tools and insights, extending beyond the scope of the methods presented in this paper, will be required to rigorously establish such extensions.

6.
Proofs of Theorems
5
,
13
,
14
and
16

In the proofs below, we always work on the event where
\|E\|\leq 2(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})
; Lemma
32
shows this event holds with probability at least
1-2e^{-(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})^{2}/2}\geq 1-2(N+n)^{-16(K+7)}
since
(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})^{2}\geq 32(K+7)\log(N+n)
by assumption.

Denote

I:=\llbracket k,s\rrbracket\cup\llbracket r+k,r+s\rrbracket

and

\displaystyle J:=\llbracket 1,2r\rrbracket\setminus I=\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket\cup\llbracket s+1,r\rrbracket\cup\llbracket r+1,r+k-1\rrbracket\cup\llbracket r+s+1,2r\rrbracket.

We first obtain an identity for the eigenvector
\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}
. For each
i\in I
, by Weyl’s inequality,
|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|\geq\widetilde{\sigma}_{r_{0}}\geq\sigma_{r_{0}}-\|E\|>\|E\|=\|\mathcal{E}\|
by supposition on
\sigma_{r_{0}}
and thus
G(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})
and
\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})
are well-defined. As
(\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{E})\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}
, we solve for
\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}
to obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}=(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}I-\mathcal{E})^{-1}\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}=G(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}.

In fact, we can approximate
G(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})
with a simpler random matrix, denoted as
\Pi(z)
below. Depending on the magnitude of
\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}
under consideration (the specifics of which will become clear in subsequent context), we choose
\Pi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})
to be either
\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})
if
|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|
is relatively small and
\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}}I_{N+n}+\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}^{2}}\mathcal{E}
or even simply
\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}}I_{N+n}
if
|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|
is sufficiently large.
Furthermore, denote

\displaystyle\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}):=G(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})-\Pi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}).

(44)

Hence, we rewrite

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}=\Pi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}+\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}

(45)

This decomposition of
\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}
is critical in facilitating the extraction of its desired property information. Note that Lemma
28
and Lemma
30
provides precise control on the size of
\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})
.

For
J\subset\llbracket 1,2r\rrbracket
, we introduce the notation
\mathcal{U}_{J}
to denote the
(N+n)\times|J|
matrix formed from
\mathcal{U}
by removing the columns containing
{\mathbf{u}}_{i}
for
i\not\in J
. Similarly,
\mathcal{D}_{J}
will denote the
|J|\times|J|
matrix formed from
\mathcal{D}
by removing the rows and columns containing
{\lambda}_{i}
for
i\not\in J
. Let
I:=\llbracket 1,2r\rrbracket\setminus J
. In this way, we can decompose
A
as

\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{U}\mathcal{D}\mathcal{U}^{T}={\mathcal{U}}_{J}{\mathcal{D}}_{J}{\mathcal{U}}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}+{\mathcal{U}}_{I}{\mathcal{D}}_{I}{\mathcal{U}}_{I}^{\mathrm{T}}.

(46)

Let
\mathcal{P}_{J}
be the orthogonal projection onto the subspace
\Span\{\mathbf{u}_{k}:{k\in J}\}
. Clearly,
\mathcal{P}_{J}=\mathcal{U}_{J}\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}.
If
J={\llbracket 1,k\rrbracket}
, we sometimes simply write
\mathcal{U}_{k}
for
\mathcal{U}_{J}
and
\mathcal{P}_{k}
for
\mathcal{P}_{J}
.
Analogous notations
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{J},\widetilde{P}_{J},\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{J}
are also defined for
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
. We also use
{\mathcal{P}}_{\llbracket 2r+1,N+n\rrbracket}={\mathcal{U}}_{\llbracket 2r+1,N+n\rrbracket}{\mathcal{U}}_{\llbracket 2r+1,N+n\rrbracket}^{\mathrm{T}}
to denote the orthogonal projection onto the null space of
\mathcal{A}
.

Now we proceed to the proofs of the main results.

6.1.
Proof of Theorem
5

From (
25
), we start by observing

\displaystyle\vvvert\sin\angle(\mathcal{U}_{I},\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I})\vvvert=\vvvert{\mathcal{P}}_{I^{c}}\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{I}\vvvert\leq\vvvert{\mathcal{P}}_{\llbracket 2r+1,N+n\rrbracket}\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{I}\vvvert+\vvvert{\mathcal{P}}_{J}\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{I}\vvvert.

We bound the two terms
\vvvert{\mathcal{P}}_{\llbracket 2r+1,N+n\rrbracket}\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{I}\vvvert
and
\vvvert{\mathcal{P}}_{J}\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{I}\vvvert
respectively.

Lemma 33
.

With probability 1,

\vvvert{\mathcal{P}}_{\llbracket 2r+1,N+n\rrbracket}\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{I}\vvvert\leq 2\frac{\vvvert\mathcal{P}_{\llbracket 2r+1,N+n\rrbracket}\mathcal{E}\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{I}\vvvert}{\sigma_{s}}.

(47)

Proof.

By Proposition
24
(i) and Theorem
21
,

\vvvert{\mathcal{P}}_{\llbracket 2r+1,N+n\rrbracket}\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{I}\vvvert=\vvvert\mathcal{U}_{\llbracket 2r+1,N+n\rrbracket}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\vvvert.

From the spectral decomposition of
\widetilde{\mathcal{A}}
, we have

\displaystyle(\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{E})\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}=\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{I}.

Multiplying by
\mathcal{U}_{\llbracket 2r+1,N+n\rrbracket}^{\mathrm{T}}
on the left of the equation above, we further have

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{\llbracket 2r+1,N+n\rrbracket}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{E}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}=\mathcal{U}_{\llbracket 2r+1,N+n\rrbracket}^{T}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{I}.

As
\sigma_{r_{0}}\geq b\|E\|\geq 2\|E\|
by supposition, Weyl’s inequality implies that

\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}\geq\sigma_{i}-\|E\|\geq\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{i}

(48)

for
k\leq i\leq s
.
Hence,
\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{I}
is invertible since
|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|=\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}>0
for
i\in\llbracket k,s\rrbracket
and
|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|=\widetilde{\sigma}_{i-r}>0
for
i\in\llbracket r+k,r+s\rrbracket
. It follows from Theorem
22
that

\displaystyle\vvvert\mathcal{U}_{\llbracket 2r+1,N+n\rrbracket}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\vvvert

\displaystyle=\vvvert\mathcal{U}_{\llbracket 2r+1,N+n\rrbracket}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{E}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{I}^{-1}\vvvert

\displaystyle\leq\vvvert\mathcal{U}_{\llbracket 2r+1,N+n\rrbracket}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{E}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\vvvert\|\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{I}^{-1}\|

\displaystyle=\frac{\vvvert\mathcal{P}_{\llbracket 2r+1,N+n\rrbracket}\mathcal{E}\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{I}\vvvert}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{s}}.

The last equation above follows from the fact that for
U
with orthonormal columns,
U^{\mathrm{T}}B
and
UU^{\mathrm{T}}B
share the same singular values.

Thus by another application of (
48
), we get

\displaystyle\vvvert{\mathcal{P}}_{\llbracket 2r+1,N+n\rrbracket}\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{I}\vvvert\leq\frac{\vvvert\mathcal{P}_{\llbracket 2r+1,N+n\rrbracket}\mathcal{E}\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{I}\vvvert}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{s}}\leq 2\frac{\vvvert\mathcal{P}_{\llbracket 2r+1,N+n\rrbracket}\mathcal{E}\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{I}\vvvert}{{\sigma}_{s}}.

(49)

as desired.
∎

It remains to bound
\vvvert{\mathcal{P}}_{J}\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{I}\vvvert=\vvvert\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\vvvert
. We apply (
24
) to obtain

\displaystyle\vvvert{\mathcal{P}}_{J}\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{I}\vvvert

\displaystyle=\vvvert\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\vvvert\leq\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\|_{*}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\rank(\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I})}\cdot\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{\min\{s-k+1,r-s+k-1\}}\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\|_{F}

\displaystyle=2\sqrt{\min\{s-k+1,r-s+k-1\}}\sqrt{\sum_{i\in I}\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|^{2}}.

(50)

In particular, for the operator norm, when
|J|\neq 0
, we simply have

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{P}}_{J}\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{I}\|=\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\|\leq\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\|_{F}=\sqrt{\sum_{i\in I}\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|^{2}}.

(51)

It remains to bound
\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|
for each
i\in I
. We have the following estimates

Lemma 34
.

For every
i\in I
,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|\leq 3\frac{(b+2)^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\frac{\eta}{\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}}

(52)

with probability at least
1-20(N+n)^{-K}
.

The proof of Lemma
34
closely mirrors the strategy employed in Lemma 20 from
[
64
]
. For the sake of completeness, we have included the proof of Lemma
34
in Appendix
C
.

It follows from (
6.1
) and Lemma
34
that

\displaystyle\vvvert{\mathcal{P}}_{J}\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{I}\vvvert\leq 6\sqrt{2}\frac{(b+2)^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\sqrt{\min\{s-k+1,r-s+k-1\}}\frac{\eta\sqrt{s-k+1}}{\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}}

with probability at least
1-20(N+n)^{-K}
.

Consequently, we arrive at

\displaystyle\vvvert\sin\angle(\mathcal{U}_{I},\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I})\vvvert=\vvvert{\mathcal{P}}_{I^{c}}\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{I}\vvvert

\displaystyle\leq 6\sqrt{2}\frac{(b+2)^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\sqrt{\min\{s-k+1,r-s+k-1\}}\frac{\eta\sqrt{s-k+1}}{\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}}

\displaystyle\quad+2\frac{\vvvert\mathcal{P}_{\llbracket 2r+1,N+n\rrbracket}\mathcal{E}\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{I}\vvvert}{\sigma_{s}}.

(53)

Specially, the following bound holds for the operator norm:

\displaystyle\|\sin\angle(\mathcal{U}_{I},\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I})\|\leq 3\sqrt{2}\frac{(b+2)^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|J|\neq 0\}}\frac{\eta\sqrt{s-k+1}}{\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}}+2\frac{\|E\|}{\sigma_{s}}.

(54)

Let
\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{2(s-k+1)}
be the principle angles of the subspaces
\mathcal{U}_{I},\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}
. Denote
\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{s-k+1}
(resp.
\beta_{1},\cdots,\beta_{s-k+1}
) the principle angles of
U_{k,s},\widetilde{U}_{k,s}
(resp.
V_{k,s},\widetilde{V}_{k,s}
). From the proof of
[
64
, Proposition 8]
, we see that the singular values of
\mathcal{U}_{I}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}
, given by
\cos\gamma_{1},\cdots,\cos\gamma_{2(s-k+1)}
, are exactly

\cos\alpha_{1},\cdots,\cos\alpha_{s-k+1},\cos\beta_{1},\cdots,\cos\beta_{s-k+1}.

Hence,

\vvvert\sin\angle(\mathcal{U}_{I},\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I})\vvvert=\vvvert\sin\angle(U_{k,s},\widetilde{U}_{k,s})\oplus\sin\angle(V_{k,s},\widetilde{V}_{k,s})\vvvert.

Note that by the definitions of
\mathcal{E},\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{I}
and
\mathcal{P}_{\llbracket 2r+1,N+n\rrbracket}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\llbracket 2r+1,N+n\rrbracket}\mathcal{E}\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{I}=\begin{pmatrix}0&P_{U^{\perp}}EP_{\widetilde{V}_{k,s}}\\
P_{V^{\perp}}E^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\widetilde{U}_{k,s}}&0\end{pmatrix}.

Using the unitary equivalence, we find

\vvvert\mathcal{P}_{\llbracket 2r+1,N+n\rrbracket}\mathcal{E}\widetilde{\mathcal{P}}_{I}\vvvert=\vvvert P_{U^{\perp}}EP_{\widetilde{V}_{k,s}}\oplus P_{V^{\perp}}E^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\widetilde{U}_{k,s}}\vvvert.

Hence, from (
6.1
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\vvvert\sin\angle(U_{k,s},\widetilde{U}_{k,s})\oplus\sin\angle(V_{k,s},\widetilde{V}_{k,s})\vvvert

\displaystyle\leq 6\sqrt{2}\frac{(b+2)^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\sqrt{\min\{s-k+1,r-s+k-1\}}\frac{\eta\sqrt{s-k+1}}{\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}}

\displaystyle\quad+2\frac{\vvvert P_{U^{\perp}}EP_{\widetilde{V}_{k,s}}\oplus P_{V^{\perp}}E^{\mathrm{T}}P_{\widetilde{U}_{k,s}}\vvvert}{{\sigma}_{s}}.

(55)

The conclusion of Theorem
5
follows immediately from the fact that

\max\{\vvvert\sin\angle(U_{k,s},\widetilde{U}_{k,s})\vvvert,\vvvert\sin\angle(V_{k,s},\widetilde{V}_{k,s})\vvvert\}\leq\vvvert\sin\angle(U_{k,s},\widetilde{U}_{k,s})\oplus\sin\angle(V_{k,s},\widetilde{V}_{k,s})\vvvert

by Theorem
23
.

Specially, for the operator norm, from (
54
), we see that

\displaystyle\max\{\|\sin\angle(U_{k,s},\widetilde{U}_{k,s})\|,\|\sin\angle(V_{k,s},\widetilde{V}_{k,s})\|\}

\displaystyle\leq\|\sin\angle(U_{k,s},\widetilde{U}_{k,s})\oplus\sin\angle(V_{k,s},\widetilde{V}_{k,s})\|=\|\sin\angle(\mathcal{U}_{I},\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I})\|

\displaystyle\leq 3\sqrt{2}\frac{(b+1)^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{\{s-k+1\neq r\}}\frac{\eta\sqrt{s-k+1}}{\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}}+2\frac{\|E\|}{{\sigma}_{s}}.

This completes the proof.

6.2.
Proof of Theorem
13

We start with the decomposition (
45
):

\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}=\Pi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}+\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}.

In the proof, we set

\displaystyle\Pi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}):=\begin{cases}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}),&\text{if }|\lambda_{i}|\leq n^{2};\\
\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}}I_{N+n},&\text{if }|\lambda_{i}|>n^{2},\end{cases}

(56)

and recall that

\displaystyle\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})=G(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})-\Pi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}).

Let
\mathcal{Q}=I-\mathcal{P}_{r}
be the orthogonal projection matrix onto the null space of
\mathcal{A}
. It is elementary to verify that
\mathcal{P}_{r}\Pi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}=\Pi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}
using the definitions of
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{P}_{r}=\mathcal{U}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}
. Hence, continuing from (
45
), we can derive the following expression

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}=\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}.

Furthermore, we obtain the decomposition

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{I}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}+\mathcal{P}_{J}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}+\mathcal{Q}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{I}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}+\mathcal{P}_{J}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}+\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}-\mathcal{P}_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}=\mathcal{P}_{J}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}+(\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i})_{i\in I}.

(57)

We aim to bound

\|\widetilde{U}_{k,s}-P_{U_{k,s}}\widetilde{U}_{k,s}\|_{2,\infty}=\max_{1\leq l\leq N}\|\mathsf{e}_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}(U_{k,s}-P_{U_{k,s}}\widetilde{U}_{k,s})\|.

where
\mathsf{e}_{l}
’s are the canonical vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{N}
. From the definition of

\mathcal{U}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}U&U\\
V&-V\end{pmatrix}

from Section
5.3
, it is elementary to check that

\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}-P_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}\widetilde{U}_{k,s}-P_{U_{k,s}}\widetilde{U}_{k,s}&\widetilde{U}_{k,s}-P_{U_{k,s}}\widetilde{U}_{k,s}\\
\widetilde{V}_{k,s}-P_{V_{k,s}}\widetilde{V}_{k,s}&-(\widetilde{V}_{k,s}-P_{V_{k,s}}\widetilde{V}_{k,s})\end{pmatrix}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{U}_{k,s}-P_{U_{k,s}}\widetilde{U}_{k,s}\|_{2,\infty}=\max_{1\leq l\leq N}\left\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}-\mathcal{P}_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\right)\right\|,

where
e_{l}
’s are the canonical vectors in
\mathbb{R}^{N+n}
. Continuing from (
57
), we see

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{U}_{k,s}-P_{U_{k,s}}\widetilde{U}_{k,s}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle=\max_{1\leq l\leq N}\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\left(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}-\mathcal{P}_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\right)\|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq l\leq N}\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{P}_{J}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\|\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{|J|\neq 0\}}+\max_{1\leq l\leq N}\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}(\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i})_{i\in I}\|.

(58)

Provided that
|J|\neq 0
or equivalently,
s-k+1\neq r
, the first term on the right-hand side of (
6.2
) can be bounded by

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq N}\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{P}_{J}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\|

\displaystyle=\max_{1\leq l\leq N}\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{U}_{J}\cdot\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq l\leq N}\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{U}_{J}\|\cdot\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|U\|_{2,\infty}\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\|_{F}=\|U\|_{2,\infty}\sqrt{\sum_{i\in I}\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|^{2}}.

By Lemma
34
, we further obtain

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq N}\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{P}_{J}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\|

\displaystyle\leq 3\sqrt{2}\frac{(b+1)^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\|U\|_{2,\infty}\frac{\eta\sqrt{s-k+1}}{\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}}

(59)

with probability at least
1-20(N+n)^{-K}
.

Next, we bound the second term on the right-hand side of (
6.2
):

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq N}\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}(\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i})_{i\in I}\|=\max_{1\leq l\leq N}\sqrt{\sum_{i\in I}(e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i})^{2}}.

(60)

For each
i\in I
,

\displaystyle\left|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}(I-\mathcal{U}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}})\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\cdot\mathcal{D}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}(I-\mathcal{U}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}})\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\right\|\cdot\left\|\mathcal{D}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\right\|.

(61)

Observe from
(\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{E})\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}=\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}
that
\mathcal{U}\mathcal{D}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}=(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}I-\mathcal{E})\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}
. Multiplying
\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}
on both sides, we get the bound

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathcal{D}}{\mathcal{U}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\right\|\leq\|\mathcal{E}\|+|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{b-1}\right)|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|=\frac{b}{b-1}|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|

(62)

using the assumption
\|\mathcal{E}\|=\|E\|\leq\frac{1}{b}|{\lambda}_{i}|
and the Weyl’s inequality
|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|\geq|{\lambda}_{i}|-\|E\|\geq(b-1)\|E\|
.

To estimate

\displaystyle\left\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}(I-\mathcal{U}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}})\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\right\|+\left\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{U}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\right\|+\left\|U\|_{2,\infty}\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\right\|,

(63)

we split the index set
I
into two disjoint sets:

\mathcal{I}_{s}:=\left\{i\in I:|\lambda_{i}|\leq n^{2}\right\}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{I}_{b}:=\left\{i\in I:|\lambda_{i}|>n^{2}\right\}.

Note that
\mathcal{I}_{s}
or
\mathcal{I}_{b}
could be the empty set.

Case (1):

i\in I\cap\mathcal{I}_{s}
. In this case,

\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})=G(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})-\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}).

Note that if
z\in S_{\sigma_{i}}
specified in (
40
) for any
1\leq i\leq r_{0}
, then
|z|\geq 2b(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})
by the supposition of
\sigma_{i}
. Recall

\eta=\frac{11b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\sqrt{(K+7)\log(N+n)+2(\log 9)r}.

We work on the event
\mathsf{E}:=\cap_{i\in\llbracket k,s\rrbracket\cap\mathcal{I}_{s}}\mathsf{E}_{i}
where

\displaystyle\mathsf{E}_{i}

\displaystyle:=\left\{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}\in S_{\sigma_{l_{i}}}\text{ for some }l_{i}\in\llbracket 1,r_{0}\rrbracket\right\}\cap\left\{\left\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\mathcal{U}\right\|\leq\frac{\eta}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}^{2}}\right\}

\displaystyle\cap\left\{\left|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}){\mathbf{u}}_{s}\right|\leq\frac{5b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\frac{\sqrt{(K+7)\log(N+n)}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}^{2}}\text{ for }1\leq l\leq N+n,1\leq s\leq r\right\}.

(64)

By Theorem
29
, Lemma
28
and Lemma
27
, the event
\mathsf{E}
holds with probability at least
1-20(N+n)^{-K}
. For
i\in\llbracket k,s\rrbracket\cap\mathcal{I}_{s}
,
\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}=\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}
. It follows immediately that

\displaystyle\left\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\right\|+\|U\|_{2,\infty}\left\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\right\|

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\sum_{s=1}^{2r}\left(e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathbf{u}_{s}\right)^{2}}+\|U\|_{2,\infty}\left\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\right\|

\displaystyle<\frac{18b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\frac{\sqrt{r(K+7)\log(N+n)}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}^{2}}(1+\|U\|_{2,\infty}).

(65)

Continuing from (
6.2
), (
62
) and (
6.2
), we further have for any
i\in I\cap\mathcal{I}_{s}
,

\left|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\right|\leq\frac{2b}{b-1}\frac{\gamma}{|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|}(1+\|U\|_{2,\infty})

(66)

where we define

\gamma:=\frac{9b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\sqrt{r(K+7)\log(N+n)}

for the sake of brevity.
For
i\in\llbracket r+k,r+s\rrbracket\cap\mathcal{I}_{s}
,
\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}=-\widetilde{\sigma}_{i-r}
. Note that
\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\sim-\Xi(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i-r})
since the distribution of
E
is symmetric. The bound (
66
) still holds.

Case (2):

i\in I\cap\mathcal{I}_{b}
. In this case,

\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})=G(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})-\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}}I_{N+n}.

By Weyl’s inequality,
|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|\geq n^{2}-\|\mathcal{E}\|\geq 4(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})
for every
i\in\mathcal{I}_{b}
, we apply Lemma
30
to get

\|\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\|\leq\frac{2\|\mathcal{E}\|}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}^{2}}.

As a result,

\displaystyle\left\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\right\|+\|U\|_{2,\infty}\left\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq(1+\|U\|_{2,\infty})\|\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\|\leq\frac{2\|\mathcal{E}\|}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}^{2}}(1+\|U\|_{2,\infty}).

Continuing from (
6.2
) and (
6.2
), we further have

\displaystyle\left|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\right|\leq\frac{2b}{b-1}\frac{\|\mathcal{E}\|}{|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|}(1+\|U\|_{2,\infty}).

(67)

Note by Weyl’s inequality, for
i\in\llbracket k,s\rrbracket
,
|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|\geq\frac{b-1}{b}\sigma_{i}
and for
i\in\llbracket r+k,r+s\rrbracket
,
|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|\geq\frac{b-1}{b}\sigma_{i-r}
. Continuing from (
60
) with (
66
) and (
67
), we conclude that

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq N}\left\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}(Q\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i})_{i\in I}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{2}\frac{b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}(1+\|U\|_{2,\infty})\sqrt{\sum_{i\in\llbracket k,s\rrbracket,\sigma_{i}\leq n^{2}}\frac{\gamma^{2}}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}+\sum_{i\in\llbracket k,s\rrbracket,\sigma_{i}>n^{2}}\frac{\|E\|^{2}}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}}.

Note that
\|E\|\leq 2(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})\leq 4\sqrt{n}
. Inserting the above estimate and (
59
) into (
6.2
) yields that

\displaystyle\left\|\widetilde{U}_{k,s}-P_{U_{k,s}}\widetilde{U}_{k,s}\right\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq 3\sqrt{2}\frac{(b+1)^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\|U\|_{2,\infty}\frac{\eta\sqrt{s-k+1}}{\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}}\mathbf{1}_{\{s-k+1\neq r\}}

\displaystyle+\frac{2\sqrt{2}b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}(1+\|U\|_{2,\infty})\sqrt{\sum_{i\in\llbracket k,s\rrbracket,\sigma_{i}\leq n^{2}}\frac{\gamma^{2}}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}+\sum_{i\in\llbracket k,s\rrbracket,\sigma_{i}>n^{2}}\frac{16n}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}}.

This concludes the proof.

6.3.
Proof of Theorem
14

The proof strategy for Theorem
14
mirrors that of Theorem
13
. We provide a brief outline below.

First, we estimate
\left\|x^{\mathrm{T}}(\widetilde{U}_{k,s}-P_{U_{k,s}}\widetilde{U}_{k,s})\right\|
for a unit vector
x\in\mathbb{R}^{N}
. Let
\mathbf{a}=(x^{\mathrm{T}},0)^{\mathrm{T}}
be a unit vector in
\mathbb{R}^{N+n}
. Following the same line of the above proof, we first observe that

\left\|x^{\mathrm{T}}(\widetilde{U}_{k,s}-P_{U_{k,s}}\widetilde{U}_{k,s})\right\|=\left\|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}-P_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I})\right\|.

Using the same proof as that of (
6.2
), one gets

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}-P_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I})\right\|\leq\|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{P}_{J}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\|\mathbf{1}_{\{|J|\neq 0\}}+\|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}(\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i})_{i\in I}\|.

(68)

For the first term on the right-hand side of (
68
), following the same line as (
59
), we have with probability at least
1-20(N+n)^{-K}
that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{P}_{J}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\|\leq 3\sqrt{2}\frac{(b+1)^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}U\|\frac{\eta\sqrt{s-k+1}}{\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}}\mathbf{1}_{\{|J|\neq 0\}}.

For the second term on the right-hand side of (
68
), using the similar arguments as (
60
), we also get with probability at least
1-20(N+n)^{-K}
that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}(\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i})_{i\in I}\|\leq\frac{2\sqrt{2}b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}(1+\|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{U}\|)\sqrt{\sum_{i=k}^{s}\frac{\gamma^{2}}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}}.

Combining the above estimates and noting that
\|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{U}\|=\|x^{\mathrm{T}}U\|
, we conclude

\displaystyle\left\|x^{\mathrm{T}}(\widetilde{U}_{k,s}-P_{U_{k,s}}\widetilde{U}_{k,s})\right\|\leq

\displaystyle 3\sqrt{2}\frac{(b+1)^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\|x^{\mathrm{T}}U\|\frac{\eta\sqrt{s-k+1}}{\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}}\mathbf{1}_{\{s-k+1\neq r\}}

\displaystyle+\frac{2\sqrt{2}b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}(1+\|x^{\mathrm{T}}U\|)\sqrt{\sum_{i=k}^{s}\frac{\gamma^{2}}{\sigma_{i}^{2}}}

with probability at least
1-40(N+n)^{-K}
.

Next, we turn to the estimation of
\left|x^{\mathrm{T}}(\widetilde{U}_{k,s}-P_{U_{k,s}}\widetilde{U}_{k,s})y\right|
. Set
\mathbf{b}=(y^{\mathrm{T}},0)^{\mathrm{T}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2(k-s+1)}
. It is elementary to check that

\displaystyle\left|x^{\mathrm{T}}(\widetilde{U}_{k,s}-P_{U_{k,s}}\widetilde{U}_{k,s})y\right|=\sqrt{2}\left|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}-P_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I})\mathbf{b}\right|.

Using the decomposition in (
57
), we get

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}-P_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I})\mathbf{b}\right|\leq\left|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{P}_{J}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\mathbf{b}\right|\mathbf{1}_{\{|J|\neq 0\}}+\left|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}(\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i})_{i\in I}\mathbf{b}\right|.

(69)

When
|J|\neq 0
, taking the definitions of
\mathbf{a},\mathbf{b}
into consideration, we can derive an upper bound for the first term on the right-hand side of equation (
69
):

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{P}_{J}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\mathbf{b}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{U}_{J}\cdot\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\mathbf{b}\right|\leq\|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{U}_{J}\|\cdot\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\mathbf{b}\|=\|x^{\mathrm{T}}U_{J_{0}}\|\left\|\sum_{i\in I_{0}}y_{i}\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\right\|

where
J_{0}:=\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket\cup\llbracket s+1,r\rrbracket
and
I_{0}:=\llbracket k,s\rrbracket
. By Lemma
34
, we further obtain for each
i\in I_{0}
,

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|\leq 3\frac{(b+1)^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\frac{\eta}{\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}}

with probability at least
1-20(N+n)^{-K}
. By Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we further obtain

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{P}_{J}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\mathbf{b}\right|\leq 3\frac{(b+1)^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\|x^{\mathrm{T}}U\|\frac{\eta\sqrt{\|y\|_{0}}}{\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}}

(70)

with probability at least
1-20(N+n)^{-K}
.

For the second term on the right-hand side of equation (
69
), we start with

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}(\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i})_{i\in I}\mathbf{b}\right|=\left|\sum_{i\in I_{0}}y_{i}\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\right|.

(71)

For each
i\in I_{0}
, similar to (
6.2
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}(I-\mathcal{U}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}})\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\cdot\mathcal{D}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}(I-\mathcal{U}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}})\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\right\|\cdot\left\|\mathcal{D}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\right\|.

Note that, by (
62
),

\left\|\mathcal{D}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\right\|\leq\frac{b}{b-1}|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|.

The estimation of

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}(I-\mathcal{U}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}})\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\right\|+\left\|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{U}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\right\|+\|x^{\mathrm{T}}U\|\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\|

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\sum_{s=1}^{2r}\left(\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathbf{u}_{s}\right)^{2}}+\|x^{\mathrm{T}}U\|\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\|

\displaystyle<\frac{18b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\frac{\sqrt{r(K+7)\log(N+n)}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}^{2}}(1+\|x^{\mathrm{T}}U\|)=\frac{2\gamma}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}^{2}}(1+\|x^{\mathrm{T}}U\|)

follows the same line as those of (
6.2
) and (
6.2
) with
e_{l}
replaced by the unit vector
\mathbf{a}
.
In particular, combined with
|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|\geq\frac{b-1}{b}\sigma_{i}
for each
i\in I_{0}
from the Weyl’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}(I-\mathcal{U}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}})\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\right\|\cdot\left\|\mathcal{D}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2b}{b-1}\frac{\gamma}{|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|}(1+\|x^{\mathrm{T}}U\|)\leq\frac{2b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\frac{\gamma}{\sigma_{i}}(1+\|x^{\mathrm{T}}U\|)

holds with probability at least
1-20(N+n)^{-K}
.
Continuing from (
71
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}(\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i})_{i\in I}\mathbf{b}\right|\leq\frac{2b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}{\gamma}(1+\|x^{\mathrm{T}}U\|)\sum_{i=k}^{s}\frac{|y_{i}|}{\sigma_{i}}.

(72)

Finally, inserting (
70
) and (
72
) back into (
69
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\left|x^{\mathrm{T}}(\widetilde{U}_{k,s}-P_{U_{k,s}}\widetilde{U}_{k,s})y\right|

\displaystyle=\sqrt{2}\left|\mathbf{a}^{\mathrm{T}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}-P_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I})\mathbf{b}\right|

\displaystyle\leq 3\sqrt{2}\frac{(b+1)^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\|x^{\mathrm{T}}U\|\frac{\eta\sqrt{\|y\|_{0}}}{\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}}\mathbf{1}_{\{s-k+1\neq r\}}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2\sqrt{2}b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}{\gamma}(1+\|x^{\mathrm{T}}U\|)\sum_{i=k}^{s}\frac{|y_{i}|}{\sigma_{i}}

holds with probability at least
1-40(N+n)^{-K}
.

6.4.
Proof of Theorem
16

The proof of Theorem
16
follows largely the proof of Theorem
13
. We sketch the proof and focus on the difference.
We start with the decomposition (
45
):

\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}=\Pi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}+\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}

and set

\displaystyle\Pi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}):=\begin{cases}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}),&\text{if }|\lambda_{i}|\leq n^{2};\\
\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}}I_{N+n}+\frac{\mathcal{E}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}^{2}},&\text{if }|\lambda_{i}|>n^{2}.\end{cases}

(73)

The definition of
\Pi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})
differs from the one given in (
56
) when
|\lambda_{i}|>n^{2}
. We adopt this definition to achieve more precise control over the error term by considering the weights
|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|
.

Let
\mathcal{Q}=I-\mathcal{P}_{r}
be the orthogonal projection matrix onto the null space of
\mathcal{A}
. Using the same derivation as in the beginning of the proof of Theorem
13
, we obtain the decomposition

\displaystyle\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}=\mathcal{P}_{I}\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}+\mathcal{P}_{J}\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}+\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}

and hence

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{I}-\mathcal{P}_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{I}=\mathcal{P}_{J}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{I}+(\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i})_{i\in I}.

(74)

It can be verified using the definitions of
\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I},\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{I}
and
\mathcal{P}_{I}=\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}^{\mathrm{T}}
that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{I}-\mathcal{P}_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{I}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\begin{pmatrix}\widetilde{U}_{k,s}\widetilde{D}_{k,s}-P_{U_{k,s}}\widetilde{U}_{k,s}\widetilde{D}_{k,s}&-(\widetilde{U}_{k,s}\widetilde{D}_{k,s}-P_{U_{k,s}}\widetilde{U}_{k,s}\widetilde{D}_{k,s})\\
\widetilde{V}_{k,s}\widetilde{D}_{k,s}-P_{V_{k,s}}\widetilde{V}_{k,s}\widetilde{D}_{k,s}&\widetilde{V}_{k,s}\widetilde{D}_{k,s}-P_{V_{k,s}}\widetilde{V}_{k,s}\widetilde{D}_{k,s}.\end{pmatrix}

Therefore, combining (
74
), we observe that

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{U}_{k,s}\widetilde{D}_{k,s}-P_{U_{k,s}}\widetilde{U}_{k,s}\widetilde{D}_{k,s}\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle=\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{I}-\mathcal{P}_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{I})\|

\displaystyle=\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}(\mathcal{P}_{J}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{I}+(\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i})_{i\in I})\|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{P}_{J}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{I}\|\mathbf{1}_{|J|\neq 0}+\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}(\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i})_{i\in I})\|.

(75)

The first term on the right-hand side of (
6.4
) can be bounded similarly as that of (
59
) using Lemma
34
: if
|J|\neq 0
, then

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq N}\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{P}_{J}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{I}\|

\displaystyle=\max_{1\leq l\leq N}\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{U}_{J}\cdot\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{I}\|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq l\leq N}\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{U}_{J}\|\cdot\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{I}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|U\|_{2,\infty}\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathcal{U}}_{I}\widetilde{\mathcal{D}}_{I}\|_{F}=\|U\|_{2,\infty}\sqrt{\sum_{i\in I}\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}^{2}\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 3\sqrt{2}\frac{(b+1)^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\|U\|_{2,\infty}\frac{\eta\sigma_{k}\sqrt{s-k+1}}{\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}}

with probability at least
1-20(N+n)^{-K}
.

The bound of the second term on the right-hand side of (
6.4
) proceeds in a similar manner to that of (
60
):

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}(\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i})_{i\in I})\|=\max_{1\leq l\leq N}\sqrt{\sum_{i\in I}\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}^{2}(e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i})^{2}}.

(76)

For each
i\in I
, we first establish the following bound using (
6.2
), (
62
) and (
6.2
):

\displaystyle|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|\cdot\left|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\right|\leq\frac{b}{b-1}\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}^{2}\left(\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\|+\|U\|_{2,\infty}\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\|\right).

(77)

We then differentiate the cases by splitting the discussion according to whether
i\in I\cap\mathcal{I}_{s}
or
i\in I\cap\mathcal{I}_{b}
, where

\mathcal{I}_{s}:=\left\{i\in I:|\lambda_{i}|\leq n^{2}\right\}\quad\text{and}\quad\mathcal{I}_{b}:=\left\{i\in I:|\lambda_{i}|>n^{2}\right\}.

If
i\in I\cap\mathcal{I}_{s}
, then by (
66
), we immediately obtain

\displaystyle|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|\cdot\left|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\right|\leq\frac{2b}{b-1}\gamma(1+\|U\|_{2,\infty})

(78)

with probability at least
1-20(N+n)^{-K}
.

If
i\in I\cap\mathcal{I}_{b}
, then the only difference from the proof of (
67
) is that

\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})=G(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})-\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}}I_{N+n}-\frac{\mathcal{E}}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}^{2}}

and by Lemma
30
, we have

\|\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\|\leq\frac{2\|\mathcal{E}\|^{2}}{|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|^{3}}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\left\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\right\|+\|U\|_{2,\infty}\left\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq(1+\|U\|_{2,\infty})\|\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\|\leq\frac{2\|\mathcal{E}\|^{2}}{|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|^{3}}(1+\|U\|_{2,\infty}).

Continuing from (
77
), we further get

\displaystyle|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|\cdot\left|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\right|\leq\frac{2b}{b-1}\frac{\|E\|^{2}}{|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|}(1+\|U\|_{2,\infty}).

(79)

Note by Weyl’s inequality, for
i\in\llbracket k,s\rrbracket
,
|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|\geq\frac{b-1}{b}\sigma_{i}
and for
i\in\llbracket r+k,r+s\rrbracket
,
|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|\geq\frac{b-1}{b}\sigma_{i-r}
. Inserting (
78
) and (
79
) back into (
76
), we obtain that with probability at least
1-20(N+n)^{-K}
,

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\|e_{l}^{\mathrm{T}}(\mathcal{Q}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i})_{i\in I})\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\sqrt{2}b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}(1+\|U\|_{2,\infty})\sqrt{\gamma^{2}(s-k+1)+\sum_{i\in\llbracket k,s\rrbracket,\sigma_{i}>n^{2}}\frac{\|E\|^{2}}{\sigma_{i}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\sqrt{2}b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}(1+\|U\|_{2,\infty})\sqrt{\gamma^{2}(s-k+1)+16},

where we used the crude estimate

\sum_{i\in\llbracket k,s\rrbracket,\sigma_{i}>n^{2}}\frac{\|E\|^{2}}{\sigma_{i}}\leq n\frac{16n}{n^{2}}=16.

This concludes the proof.

7.
Proofs of Theorems
8
,
9
and
10

7.1.
Proof of Theorem
8

By the min-max theorem, for an
N\times n
matrix
S
, the
j
th largest singular value of
S
is

\displaystyle\sigma_{j}(S)=\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}W\in\mathbb{R}^{N},\dim(W)=j\\
K\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\dim(K)=j\end{subarray}}\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,y)\in W\times K\\
\|x\|=\|y\|=1\end{subarray}}x^{T}Sy.

(80)

For the lower bound (
9
) of
\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}
, by (
80
),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}\geq\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,y)\in U_{k}\times V_{k}\\
\|x\|=\|y\|=1\end{subarray}}x^{T}(A+E)y\geq\sigma_{k}-\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,y)\in U_{k}\times V_{k}\\
\|x\|=\|y\|=1\end{subarray}}|x^{T}Ey|.

Note that

\displaystyle\|U_{k}^{T}EV_{k}\|=\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,y)\in U_{k}\times V_{k}\\
\|x\|=\|y\|=1\end{subarray}}|x^{T}Ey|.

Our assumption immediately yields that
\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}\geq\sigma_{k}-t
with probability at least
1-\varepsilon
.

For the upper bound (
10
) of
\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}
, by (
80
),

\displaystyle\sigma_{k}\geq\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}-\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,y)\in\widetilde{U}_{k}\times\widetilde{V}_{k}\\
\|x\|=\|y\|=1\end{subarray}}|x^{T}Ey|.

(81)

It is enough to bound the second term on the right side. For any unit vectors
x\in\widetilde{U}_{k}
and
y\in\widetilde{V}_{k}
, we decompose

x=P_{U^{\perp}}x+UU^{T}x
and
y=P_{V^{\perp}}y+VV^{T}\widetilde{y}.
It follows from triangle inequality that

|x^{T}Ey|\leq\|P_{U^{\perp}}x\|\cdot\|P_{V^{\perp}}y\|\cdot\|E\|+(\|P_{U^{\perp}}x\|+\|P_{V^{\perp}}y\|)\|E\|+\|U^{T}EV\|.

To bound the term
\|P_{U^{\perp}}x\|
, first notice by Cauchy-Schwartz inequality,

\max_{x\in\widetilde{U}_{k},\|x\|=1}\|P_{U^{\perp}}x\|\leq\sqrt{k}\max_{1\leq s\leq k}\|P_{U^{\perp}}\widetilde{u}_{s}\|.

Next, multiplying
P_{U^{\perp}}
on the left side of the equation
(A+E)\widetilde{v}_{s}=\widetilde{\sigma}_{s}\widetilde{u}_{s}
, we get
P_{U^{\perp}}E\widetilde{v}_{s}=\widetilde{\sigma}_{s}P_{U^{\perp}}\widetilde{u}_{s}.
It follows that

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq s\leq k}\|P_{U^{\perp}}\widetilde{u}_{s}\|\leq{\|E\|}/{\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}}

(82)

and thus

\max_{x\in\widetilde{U}_{k},\|x\|=1}\|P_{U^{\perp}}x\|\leq\sqrt{k}{\|E\|}/{\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}}.

The same calculation leads to

\max_{y\in\widetilde{V}_{k},\|y\|=1}\|P_{V^{\perp}}y\|\leq\sqrt{k}{\|E\|}/{\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}(x,y)\in\widetilde{U}_{k}\times\widetilde{V}_{k}\\
\|x\|=\|y\|=1\end{subarray}}|x^{T}Ey|\leq 2\sqrt{k}\frac{\|E\|^{2}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}}+k\frac{\|E\|^{3}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}^{2}}+\|U^{T}EV\|.

(83)

Since
\|U^{T}EV\|\leq L
and
\|E\|\leq B
with probability at least
1-\varepsilon
, by (
81
) and (
83
), we obtain, with probability at least
1-\varepsilon

\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}\leq\sigma_{k}+2\sqrt{k}\frac{B^{2}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}}+k\frac{B^{3}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}^{2}}+L.

7.2.
Proof of Theorem
9

In the proof, we work on the event

\Omega:=\{\|E\|\leq B\text{ and }\|U^{T}EV\|\leq L\}.

By the supposition,
\Omega
holds with probability at least
1-\varepsilon
.
Observe that
\|U_{k}^{T}EV_{k}\|\leq\|U^{T}EV\|\leq L.

Using (
9
), the lower bound for
\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}
, together with
\delta_{k}\geq 2L
, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}\geq\sigma_{k}-L\geq{\sigma_{k}}/{2}>0

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}-\sigma_{k+1}={\widetilde{\sigma}}_{k}-\sigma_{k}+\delta_{k}\geq\delta_{k}-L\geq{\delta_{k}}/{2}.

From (
25
) and triangle inequality, we see

\displaystyle\vvvert\sin\angle(U_{k},\widetilde{U}_{k})\vvvert=\vvvert P_{U_{k}^{\perp}}P_{\widetilde{U}_{k}}\vvvert\leq\vvvert P_{U^{\perp}}P_{\widetilde{U}_{k}}\vvvert+\mathbf{1}_{\{k<r\}}\vvvert P_{U_{k+1,r}}P_{\widetilde{U}_{k}}\vvvert.

(84)

We first bound the first term on the right-hand side of (
84
). Suppose
P_{U^{\perp}}=U_{0}{U_{0}}^{\mathrm{T}}
where the columns of
U_{0}
are an orthonormal basis of the subspace
U^{\perp}
. Then

\displaystyle\vvvert P_{U^{\perp}}P_{\widetilde{U}_{k}}\vvvert=\vvvert U_{0}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{k}\vvvert.

Multiplying
U_{0}^{\mathrm{T}}
on the both sides of
(A+E)\widetilde{V}_{k}=\widetilde{U}_{k}\widetilde{D}_{k}
, we see
U_{0}^{\mathrm{T}}E\widetilde{V}_{k}=U_{0}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{k}\widetilde{D}_{k}
and hence,
U_{0}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{k}=U_{0}^{\mathrm{T}}E\widetilde{V}_{k}\widetilde{D}_{k}^{-1}.
It follows that

\displaystyle\vvvert P_{U^{\perp}}P_{\widetilde{U}_{k}}\vvvert=\vvvert U_{0}^{\mathrm{T}}E\widetilde{V}_{k}\widetilde{D}_{k}^{-1}\vvvert\leq\vvvert U_{0}^{\mathrm{T}}E\widetilde{V}_{k}\vvvert\|\widetilde{D}_{k}^{-1}\|=\frac{\vvvert P_{U^{\perp}}EP_{\widetilde{V}_{k}}\vvvert}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}}.

(85)

In particular, for the operator norm, we have

\displaystyle\|P_{U^{\perp}}P_{\widetilde{U}_{k}}\|\leq\frac{\|E\|}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}}.

(86)

We proceed to bound the second term on the right-hand side of (
84
). It suffices to consider
k<r
. Observe that

\displaystyle\vvvert P_{U_{k+1,r}}P_{\widetilde{U}_{k}}\vvvert=\vvvert{U_{k+1,r}}^{\mathrm{T}}{\widetilde{U}_{k}}\vvvert

\displaystyle\leq\|{U_{k+1,r}}^{\mathrm{T}}{\widetilde{U}_{k}}\|_{*}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\rank({U_{k+1,r}}^{\mathrm{T}}{\widetilde{U}_{k}})}\|{U_{k+1,r}}^{\mathrm{T}}{\widetilde{U}_{k}}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\min\{k,r-k\}}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{k}\|{U_{k+1,r}}^{\mathrm{T}}{\widetilde{u}_{i}}\|^{2}}.

Specially, for the operator norm, we have

\displaystyle\|P_{U_{k+1,r}}P_{\widetilde{U}_{k}}\|\leq\|{U_{k+1,r}}^{\mathrm{T}}{\widetilde{U}_{k}}\|_{F}=\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{k}\|{U_{k+1,r}}^{\mathrm{T}}{\widetilde{u}_{i}}\|^{2}}.

It remains to bound
\|U_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}{\widetilde{u}_{i}}\|
for
1\leq i\leq k
. Multiplying
U_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}
on both sides of
(A+E)\widetilde{v}_{i}=\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}\widetilde{u}_{i}
, we get

D_{k+1,r}V_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{v}_{i}+U_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}E\widetilde{v}_{i}=\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}U_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{i},

which yields

\displaystyle\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}D_{k+1,r}V_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{v}_{i}=\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}^{2}U_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{i}-\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}U_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}E\widetilde{v}_{i}.

(87)

Similarly, multiplying
V_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}
on both sides of
(A^{\mathrm{T}}+E^{\mathrm{T}})\widetilde{u}_{i}=\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}\widetilde{v}_{i}
, we also get

D_{k+1,r}U_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{i}+V_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}E^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{i}=\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}V_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{v}_{i},

which implies

\displaystyle\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}D_{k+1,r}V_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{v}_{i}=D_{k+1,r}^{2}U_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{i}+D_{k+1,r}V_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}E^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{i}.

(88)

Combining (
87
) and (
88
), one has

(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}^{2}I-D_{k+1,r}^{2})U_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{i}=\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}U_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}E\widetilde{v}_{i}+D_{k+1,r}V_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}E^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{i}.

As a result, by noting
\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}^{2}I-D_{k+1,r}^{2}=\diag(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{k+1}^{2},\cdots,\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{r}^{2})
, we obtain the following bound

\displaystyle\|U_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{i}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}\|U_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}E\widetilde{v}_{i}\|+\sigma_{k+1}\|V_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}E^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{i}\|}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}^{2}-\sigma_{k+1}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\max\{\|U_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}E\widetilde{v}_{i}\|,\|V_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}E^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{i}\|\}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}-\sigma_{k+1}}.

(89)

Now we turn to bound the numerator of the above expression. Decompose

\widetilde{u}_{i}=P_{U}\widetilde{u}_{i}+P_{U^{\perp}}\widetilde{u}_{i}
. Then

V_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}E^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{i}=V_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}E^{\mathrm{T}}UU^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{i}+V_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}E^{\mathrm{T}}P_{U^{\perp}}\widetilde{u}_{i}

and

\displaystyle\|V_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}E^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{i}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|V_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}E^{\mathrm{T}}U\|+\|E^{\mathrm{T}}\|\cdot\|P_{U^{\perp}}\widetilde{u}_{i}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|U^{\mathrm{T}}EV\|+\frac{\|E\|^{2}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}}.

The last inequality above follows from
\|P_{U^{\perp}}\widetilde{u}_{i}\|\leq\|P_{U^{\perp}}P_{\widetilde{U}_{k}}\|
and by applying (
86
). Likewise, we also have

\displaystyle\|U_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}E\widetilde{v}_{i}\|\leq\|U^{\mathrm{T}}EV\|+\frac{\|E\|^{2}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}}.

Continuing from (
7.2
), we see

\displaystyle\|U_{k+1,r}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{u}_{i}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|U^{\mathrm{T}}EV\|+{\|E\|^{2}}/{\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}-\sigma_{k+1}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\frac{\|U^{\mathrm{T}}EV\|}{\delta_{k}}+4\frac{\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{k}\sigma_{k}}

by plugging in
\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}-\sigma_{k+1}\geq\delta_{k}/2
and
\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}\geq\sigma_{k}/2
.
Consequently,

\displaystyle\vvvert P_{U_{k+1,r}}P_{\widetilde{U}_{k}}\vvvert

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\min\{k,r-k\}}\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{k}\|{U_{k+1,r}}^{\mathrm{T}}{\widetilde{u}_{i}}\|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{k\min\{k,r-k\}}\left(\frac{\|U^{\mathrm{T}}EV\|}{\delta_{k}}+2\frac{\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{k}\sigma_{k}}\right).

In particular, for the operator norm,

\displaystyle\|P_{U_{k+1,r}}P_{\widetilde{U}_{k}}\|\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{k}\|{U_{k+1,r}}^{\mathrm{T}}{\widetilde{u}_{i}}\|^{2}}\leq 2\sqrt{k}\left(\frac{\|U^{\mathrm{T}}EV\|}{\delta_{k}}+2\frac{\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{k}\sigma_{k}}\right).

Combining the above estimates with (
85
), and considering (
84
), we ultimately arrive at

\displaystyle\vvvert\sin\angle(U_{k},\widetilde{U}_{k})\vvvert

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{k\min\{k,r-k\}}\left(\frac{\|U^{\mathrm{T}}EV\|}{\delta_{k}}+2\frac{\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{k}\sigma_{k}}\right)+\frac{\vvvert P_{U^{\perp}}EP_{\widetilde{V}_{k}}\vvvert}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sqrt{k\min\{k,r-k\}}\left(\frac{\|U^{\mathrm{T}}EV\|}{\delta_{k}}+2\frac{\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{k}\sigma_{k}}\right)+2\frac{k\|E\|}{\sigma_{k}}.

More specifically, for the operator norm, we have

\displaystyle\|\sin\angle(U_{k},\widetilde{U}_{k})\|\leq 2\sqrt{k}\left(\frac{\|U^{\mathrm{T}}EV\|}{\delta_{k}}+2\frac{\|E\|^{2}}{\delta_{k}\sigma_{k}}\right)\mathbf{1}_{\{k<r\}}+2\frac{\|E\|}{\sigma_{k}}.

By applying the result to
A^{\mathrm{T}}
and
A^{\mathrm{T}}+E^{\mathrm{T}}
, we observe that the same bounds also hold for
\sin\angle(V_{k},\widetilde{V}_{k})
. This concludes the proof.

7.3.
Proof of Theorem
10

We first bound
\|U^{\mathrm{T}}EV\|
. By a standard
\epsilon
-net argument (for example, Exercise 4.4.3. from
[
70
]
), for any
\frac{1}{4}
-nets
\mathcal{N}_{1}
and
\mathcal{N}_{2}
of the unit sphere in
\mathbb{R}^{r}
, we have

\|U^{\mathrm{T}}EV\|\leq 2\sup_{x\in\mathcal{N}_{1},y\in\mathcal{N}_{2}}x^{\mathrm{T}}U^{\mathrm{T}}EVy.

Besides,
|\mathcal{N}_{i}|\leq 9^{r}
for
i=1,2
(see
[
70
, Corollary 4.2.13.]
). Note that for every
x\in\mathcal{N}_{1}
and
y\in\mathcal{N}_{2}
, by Cauchy-Schwartz inequality,

|x^{\mathrm{T}}U^{\mathrm{T}}EVy|=\left|\sum_{i,j=1}^{r}x_{i}y_{j}u_{i}^{\mathrm{T}}Ev_{j}\right|\leq\sum_{i,j=1}^{r}|x_{i}y_{j}|\max_{1\leq i,j\leq r}\left|u_{i}^{\mathrm{T}}Ev_{j}\right|\leq r\max_{1\leq i,j\leq r}|u_{i}^{\mathrm{T}}Ev_{j}|.

Hence, for
t>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\|U^{\mathrm{T}}EV\|\geq t)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{x\in\mathcal{N}_{1},y\in\mathcal{N}_{2}}x^{\mathrm{T}}U^{\mathrm{T}}EVy\geq\frac{t}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 9^{2r}\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{1\leq i,j\leq r}|u_{i}^{\mathrm{T}}Ev_{j}|\geq\frac{t}{2}\frac{1}{r}\right)

\displaystyle\leq r^{2}9^{2r}f\left(\frac{t}{2r}\right).

Likewise, we also get that for
t_{0}>0
,

\mathbb{P}(\|U_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}EV_{k}\|\leq t_{0})\geq 1-k^{2}9^{2k}f\left(\frac{t_{0}}{2k}\right).

Conditioning on
\{\|U_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}EV_{k}\|\leq t_{0}=\delta_{k}/2\}
, we can apply (
9
) from Theorem
8
to obtain

\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}-\sigma_{k+1}\geq\sigma_{k}-\sigma_{k+1}-t_{0}=\delta_{k}-t_{0}=\delta_{k}/2.

Hence, the event

\Omega_{0}:=\{\|U^{\mathrm{T}}EV\|\leq t\}\cap\{\widetilde{\sigma}_{k}-\sigma_{k+1}\geq\delta_{k}/2\}

holds with probability at least

1-r^{2}9^{2r}f\left(\frac{t}{2r}\right)-k^{2}9^{2k}f\left(\frac{\delta_{k}}{4k}\right).

Going through the same lines of proof of Theorem
9
on the event
\Omega_{0}
, we prove the theorem.

8.
Proof of Theorem
18

We consider the model (
16
) and rewrite

\mathbb{E}(X)=(\theta_{z_{1}},\cdots,\theta_{z_{n}})=(\theta_{1},\cdots,\theta_{k})Z^{\mathrm{T}},

where
Z\in\{0,1\}^{n\times k}
with entry
Z_{ij}=1
if
z_{i}=j
and
Z_{ij}=0
otherwise. It is clear that the information regarding the cluster labels
\mathbf{z}
is entirely encoded within
Z
. Additionally, let
D=\diag(d_{1},\cdots,d_{k})
where
d_{i}
represents the cluster size associated with center
\theta_{i}
. Consequently, the matrix
ZD^{-1/2}
has orthonormal columns.

Given that
\theta_{i}
’s could be colinear, the rank of
\mathbb{E}(X)
or
(\theta_{1},\cdots,\theta_{k})
, denoted as
r
, could be smaller than the number of clusters
k
. Consider the SVD of

(\theta_{1},\cdots,\theta_{k})D^{1/2}=U\Lambda{W}^{\mathrm{T}},

where
\Lambda
is a
k\times k
diagonal matrix with rank
r
and
{W}
is a
k\times k
orthogonal matrix. Observe that if we denote the SVD of
\mathbb{E}(X)
as
\mathbb{E}(X)=U\Sigma V^{\mathrm{T}}
with
U\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times k}
and
V\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k}
, then the following relationship emerges:

\mathbb{E}(X)=(\theta_{1},\cdots,\theta_{k})D^{1/2}(ZD^{-1/2})^{\mathrm{T}}=U\Lambda(ZD^{-1/2}W)^{\mathrm{T}}=U\Sigma V^{\mathrm{T}}.

Therefore,
\Sigma=\Lambda=\diag(\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{r},0,\cdots,0)
and

V=ZD^{-1/2}W
. Note that the choice of
U,V
is not unique and we can only decide
U
or
V
up to an orthogonal transformation.

Next, we show that the geometric relationship among the columns of
\mathbb{E}(X)
is preserved among the columns of
U^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbb{E}(X)
. Consider the SVD of
\mathbb{E}(X)=U\Sigma V^{\mathrm{T}}
, where each column
\theta_{j}
of
\mathbb{E}(X)
can be expressed as
\theta_{j}=U\Sigma(V^{\mathrm{T}})_{j}
by denoting
(V^{\mathrm{T}})_{j}
as the column of
V^{\mathrm{T}}
. Let
(U^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbb{E}(X))_{j}
represent the columns of
U^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbb{E}(X)=(U^{\mathrm{T}}\theta_{z_{1}},\cdots,U^{\mathrm{T}}\theta_{z_{n}})
. For any two columns
\theta_{i}
and
\theta_{j}
of
\mathbb{E}(X)
, we have

\displaystyle\|\theta_{i}-\theta_{j}\|^{2}=(\theta_{i}-\theta_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}(\theta_{i}-\theta_{j})=\left((V^{\mathrm{T}})_{i}-(V^{\mathrm{T}})_{j}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\Sigma^{2}\left((V^{\mathrm{T}})_{i}-(V^{\mathrm{T}})_{j}\right)

Moreover, their corresponding columns
(U^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbb{E}(X))_{i}
and
(U^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbb{E}(X))_{j}
of
U^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbb{E}(X)
satisfy

\displaystyle\|(U^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbb{E}(X))_{i}-(U^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbb{E}(X))_{j}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\|U^{\mathrm{T}}\theta_{i}-U^{\mathrm{T}}\theta_{j}\|^{2}=(\theta_{i}-\theta_{j})^{\mathrm{T}}UU^{\mathrm{T}}(\theta_{i}-\theta_{j})

\displaystyle=\left((V^{\mathrm{T}})_{i}-(V^{\mathrm{T}})_{j}\right)^{\mathrm{T}}\Sigma U^{\mathrm{T}}UU^{\mathrm{T}}U\Sigma\left((V^{\mathrm{T}})_{i}-(V^{\mathrm{T}})_{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\|\theta_{i}-\theta_{j}\|^{2}.

It follows that

\|(U^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbb{E}(X))_{i}-(U^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbb{E}(X))_{j}\|=\|\theta_{i}-\theta_{j}\|.

Therefore, if
i,j\in[n]
belong to the same cluster, then
\|(U^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbb{E}(X))_{i}-(U^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbb{E}(X))_{j}\|=0
. On the other hand, if
i,j\in[n]
belong to the distinct clusters, then
\|(U^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbb{E}(X))_{i}-(U^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbb{E}(X))_{j}\|\geq\Delta.

The main step of the proof, as explained in Section
4.1
, is to prove (
17
). Specifically, we aim to show that

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq j\leq n}\|(\widetilde{U}_{k}^{T}X)_{j}-(U^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbb{E}(X))_{j}\|<\frac{1}{5}\Delta

(90)

holds with high probability.

Recall that throughout the paper, we always assume
\|E\|\leq 2(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{p})
. We start with the decompositions

U^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbb{E}(X)=\Lambda V^{\mathrm{T}}=\begin{pmatrix}\Lambda_{r}V_{r}^{\mathrm{T}}\\
0\end{pmatrix}

and

\widetilde{U}_{r}^{T}X={\widetilde{\Lambda}_{k}}{\widetilde{V}_{k}^{\mathrm{T}}}=\begin{pmatrix}{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{r}}{\widetilde{V}_{r}^{\mathrm{T}}}\\
{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\llbracket r+1,k\rrbracket}}{\widetilde{V}_{\llbracket r+1,k\rrbracket}^{\mathrm{T}}}\end{pmatrix}.

Observe that

\|{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\llbracket r+1,k\rrbracket}}{\widetilde{V}_{\llbracket r+1,k\rrbracket}^{\mathrm{T}}}\|\leq\widetilde{\sigma}_{r+1}\leq\|E\|\leq\frac{1}{20}\Delta

by Weyl’s inequality and
\Delta\geq\sigma_{r}\geq 20\|E\|
from the supposition. Hence,

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq j\leq n}\|(\widetilde{U}_{k}^{T}X)_{j}-(U^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbb{E}(X))_{j}\|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq j\leq n}\left(\|({\widetilde{\Lambda}_{r}}{\widetilde{V}_{r}^{\mathrm{T}}})_{j}-(\Lambda_{r}V_{r}^{\mathrm{T}})_{j}\|+\|({\widetilde{\Lambda}_{\llbracket r+1,k\rrbracket}}{\widetilde{V}_{\llbracket r+1,k\rrbracket}^{\mathrm{T}}})_{j}\|\right)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq j\leq n}\|({\widetilde{\Lambda}_{r}}{\widetilde{V}_{r}^{\mathrm{T}}})_{j}-(\Lambda_{r}V_{r}^{\mathrm{T}})_{j}\|+\frac{1}{20}\Delta

\displaystyle=\max_{1\leq j\leq n}\|e_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}{\widetilde{V}_{r}}{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{r}}-e_{j}^{\mathrm{T}}V_{r}\Lambda_{r}\|+\frac{1}{20}\Delta=\|{\widetilde{V}_{r}}{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{r}}-V_{r}\Lambda_{r}\|_{2,\infty}+\frac{1}{20}\Delta

\displaystyle\leq\|{\widetilde{V}_{r}}{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{r}}-V_{r}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{r}\|_{2,\infty}+\|V_{r}(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{r}-\Lambda_{r})\|_{2,\infty}+\frac{1}{20}\Delta.

(91)

Due to non-uniqueness of the choice of
V
in the SVD of
\mathbb{E}(X)
, we choose a specified
V_{r}
such that the conclusion of Corollary
17
holds (with
b=20
): that is, with probability at least
1-40(N+n)^{-L}
,

\|\widetilde{V}_{r}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{r}-V_{r}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{r}\|_{2,\infty}\leq 45r\sqrt{(L+7)\log(n+p)}(1+\|V_{r}\|_{2,\infty})+8\|V_{r}\|_{2,\infty}\frac{(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{p})^{2}}{\sigma_{r}}.

Note that

\displaystyle\|V_{r}\|_{2,\infty}\leq\|V\|_{2,\infty}=\max_{i}\|e_{i}^{\mathrm{T}}V\|=\max_{i}\sqrt{\frac{1}{d_{z_{i}}}\sum_{j=1}^{k}W_{z_{i},j}^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{c_{\min}}}\leq 1.

Continuing from (
8
), we further obtain

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq j\leq n}\|(\widetilde{U}_{k}^{T}X)_{j}-(U^{\mathrm{T}}\mathbb{E}(X))_{j}\|

\displaystyle\leq\|{\widetilde{V}_{r}}{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{r}}-V_{r}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{r}\|_{2,\infty}+\|V_{r}\|_{2,\infty}\|\widetilde{\Lambda}_{r}-\Lambda_{r}\|+\frac{1}{20}\Delta

\displaystyle\leq 90k\sqrt{(L+7)\log(n+p)}+\frac{8(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{p})^{2}}{\sqrt{c_{\min}}\sigma_{r}}+\frac{2(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{p})}{\sqrt{c_{\min}}}+\frac{1}{20}\Delta

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{20}\Delta+\frac{1}{200}\Delta+\frac{1}{20}\Delta+\frac{1}{20}\Delta<\frac{1}{5}\Delta

by Weyl’s inequality
\|\widetilde{\Lambda}_{r}-\Lambda_{r}\|\leq\|E\|
and the suppositions that
\sigma_{r}\geq 40(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{p})
and

\Delta\geq\max\left\{\frac{40(\sqrt{n}+\sqrt{p})}{\sqrt{c_{\min}}},1800k\sqrt{(L+7)\log(n+p)}\right\}.

This concludes the proof.

Appendix A
Proofs of (
26
), (
27
), and Proposition
25

A.1.
Proof of (
26
)

It follows from (
23
) that for the Schatten
p
-norm with
p\geq 2
,

\displaystyle\min_{O\in\mathbb{O}^{r\times r}}\|UO-V\|_{p}^{2}

\displaystyle=\min_{O\in\mathbb{O}^{r\times r}}\|(UO-V)^{\mathrm{T}}(UO-V)\|_{p/2}

\displaystyle=\min_{O\in\mathbb{O}^{r\times r}}\|2I_{r}-O^{\mathrm{T}}U^{\mathrm{T}}V-V^{\mathrm{T}}UO\|_{p/2}.

(92)

Note that the SVD of
U^{\mathrm{T}}V
can be written as
O_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}\cos\Theta O_{2}
where
O_{1},O_{2}
are orthogonal matrices and
\cos\Theta:=\cos\angle(U,V)=\diag(\cos\theta_{1},\cdots,\cos\theta_{r})
. Continuing from (
A.1
), by the definition of unitarily invariant norms, we further have

(
A.1
)

\displaystyle=\min_{O\in\mathbb{O}^{r\times r}}\|2I_{r}-\cos\Theta\cdot O_{2}O^{\mathrm{T}}O_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}-O_{1}OO_{2}^{\mathrm{T}}\cos\Theta\|_{p/2}

\displaystyle\leq\|2I_{r}-2\cos\Theta\|_{p/2}=2\left(\sum_{i=1}^{r}(1-\cos\theta_{i})^{p/2}\right)^{2/p}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\sum_{i=1}^{r}\sin^{p}\theta_{i}\right)^{2/p}=2\|\sin\Theta\|_{p}^{2},

where we denote
\sin\Theta:=\sin\angle(U,V)
. In the first inequality, we choose
O=O_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}O_{2}
. The second inequality follows from
1-\cos\theta_{i}\leq 1-\cos^{2}\theta_{i}=\sin^{2}\theta_{i}.

For the lower bound, we still consider

(
A.1
)

\displaystyle=\min_{O\in\mathbb{O}^{r\times r}}\|2I_{r}-\cos\Theta\cdot O_{2}O^{\mathrm{T}}O_{1}^{\mathrm{T}}-O_{1}OO_{2}^{\mathrm{T}}\cos\Theta\|_{p/2}

\displaystyle=\min_{Y\in\mathbb{O}^{r\times r}}\|2I_{r}-\cos\Theta\cdot Y-Y^{\mathrm{T}}\cos\Theta\|_{p/2}:=\min_{Y\in\mathbb{O}^{r\times r}}\|B_{Y}\|_{p/2}.

Observe that
B_{Y}
is positive semidefinite. To see this, let
x
be an arbitrary unit vector in
\mathbb{R}^{r}
and we have

\displaystyle x^{\mathrm{T}}B_{Y}x=2-2x^{\mathrm{T}}\cos\Theta\cdot Yx

\displaystyle\geq 2(1-|x^{\mathrm{T}}\cos\Theta\cdot Yx|)\geq 2(1-\|\cos\Theta\|)\geq 0.

Denote
p^{\prime}=p/2
for brevity. We use the following variational formula for the Schatten norms of positive semidefinite matrices:

\displaystyle\|B_{Y}\|_{p^{\prime}}=\max_{\|X\|_{q^{\prime}}\leq 1}\tr(B_{Y}X),

(93)

where
\|X\|_{q^{\prime}}
is the Schatten-
q^{\prime}
norm of
X\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
and
\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}=1
. To prove (
93
), by the Hölder’s inequality for Schatten norms,

\max_{\|X\|_{q^{\prime}}\leq 1}\tr(B_{Y}X)\leq\|B_{Y}\|_{p^{\prime}}\max_{\|X\|_{q^{\prime}}\leq 1}\|X\|_{q^{\prime}}\leq\|B_{Y}\|_{p^{\prime}}.

On the other hand, taking
X=B_{Y}^{p^{\prime}-1}/\|B_{Y}^{p^{\prime}-1}\|_{q^{\prime}}
,

\max_{\|X\|_{q^{\prime}}\leq 1}\tr(B_{Y}X)\geq\tr(B_{Y}^{p^{\prime}})/\|B_{Y}^{p^{\prime}-1}\|_{q^{\prime}}=\|B_{Y}\|_{p^{\prime}}^{p^{\prime}}/\|B_{Y}\|_{p^{\prime}}^{p^{\prime}-1}=\|B_{Y}\|_{p^{\prime}},

where we used
\tr(B_{Y}^{p^{\prime}})=\|B_{Y}\|_{p^{\prime}}^{p^{\prime}}
since
B_{Y}
is positive semidefinite and
\|B_{Y}^{p^{\prime}-1}\|_{q^{\prime}}=\|B_{Y}\|_{p^{\prime}}^{p^{\prime}-1}
due to
\frac{1}{p^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{q^{\prime}}=1
. This proves (
93
).

Set
S=\sin^{2}\Theta
for simplicity and let

X=\frac{S^{p^{\prime}-1}}{\|S^{p^{\prime}-1}\|_{q^{\prime}}}=\frac{S^{p^{\prime}-1}}{\|S\|_{p^{\prime}}^{p^{\prime}-1}}.

We continue from (
93
):

\displaystyle\|B_{Y}\|_{p^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\geq\tr\left(2X-\cos\Theta\cdot YX-Y^{\mathrm{T}}\cos\Theta X\right)=2\tr(X-X\cos\Theta\cdot Y)

\displaystyle\geq 2\left(\tr(X)-\|X\cos\Theta\|_{*}\right),

where we applied the Hölder’s inequality
\left|\tr(X\cos\Theta\cdot Y)\right|\leq\|Y\|\cdot\|X\cos\Theta\|_{*}=\|X\cos\Theta\|_{*}.
Plugging in
X
and
S
, we get

\displaystyle\|B_{Y}\|_{p^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{2}{\|S\|_{p^{\prime}}^{p^{\prime}-1}}\left(\tr(S^{p^{\prime}-1})-\|S^{p^{\prime}-1}\cos\Theta\|_{*}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\|\sin^{2}\Theta\|_{p^{\prime}}^{p^{\prime}-1}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{r}(\sin\theta_{i})^{2(p^{\prime}-1)}(1-\cos\theta_{i})\right).

Note that
\|\sin^{2}\Theta\|_{p^{\prime}}^{p^{\prime}-1}=\|\sin\Theta\|_{2p^{\prime}}^{2(p^{\prime}-1)}
and

1-\cos\theta_{i}=2\sin^{2}(\theta_{i}/2)=\frac{\sin^{2}\theta_{i}}{2\cos^{2}(\theta_{i}/2)}\geq\frac{1}{2}\sin^{2}\theta_{i}.

We further obtain

\displaystyle\|B_{Y}\|_{p^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\|\sin\Theta\|_{2p^{\prime}}^{2(p^{\prime}-1)}}\sum_{i=1}^{r}(\sin\theta_{i})^{2p^{\prime}}=\frac{\|\sin\Theta\|_{2p^{\prime}}^{2p^{\prime}}}{\|\sin\Theta\|_{2p^{\prime}}^{2(p^{\prime}-1)}}=\|\sin\Theta\|_{2p^{\prime}}^{2}=\|\sin\Theta\|_{p}^{2}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\eqref{eq:upsp}=\min_{Y\in\mathbb{O}^{r\times r}}\|B_{Y}\|_{p/2}\geq\|\sin\Theta\|_{p}^{2}.

This completes the proof of (
26
).

A.2.
Proof of (
27
)

For simplicity, denote
\cos\Theta=\cos\angle(U,V)
and
\sin\Theta=\sin\angle(U,V)
. Note that for any orthogonal matrices
Y,Z\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
,

\vvvert UYZ^{\mathrm{T}}-V\vvvert=\vvvert UY-VZ\vvvert.

We use
[
21
, Theorem VII.1.8]
: there exist
r\times r
orthogonal matrices
Y,Z
and
n\times n
orthogonal matrix
Q
such that if
2r\leq n
, then

QUY=\begin{pmatrix}I_{r}\\
0\\
0\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{and}\quad QUZ=\begin{pmatrix}\cos\Theta\\
\sin\Theta\\
0\end{pmatrix}.

Hence,

\min_{O\in\mathbb{O}^{r\times r}}\vvvert UO-V\vvvert\leq\vvvert UYZ^{\mathrm{T}}-V\vvvert=\vvvert QUY-QVZ\vvvert=\left\vvvert\begin{pmatrix}I_{r}-\cos\Theta\\
-\sin\Theta\end{pmatrix}\right\vvvert.

If
2r>n
, then

QUY=\begin{pmatrix}I_{n-r}&0\\
0&I_{2r-n}\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{and}\quad QUZ=\begin{pmatrix}\cos\Theta_{1}&0\\
0&I_{2r-n}\\
\sin\Theta_{1}&0\end{pmatrix},

where
\Theta_{1}
is a diagonal matrix composed of the largest
n-r
diagonal entries of
\Theta
(note that the remaining diagonal entries of
\Theta
are all zero). Therefore, by unitary equivalent, we still have

\min_{O\in\mathbb{O}^{r\times r}}\vvvert UO-V\vvvert\leq\vvvert UYZ^{\mathrm{T}}-V\vvvert=\vvvert QUY-QVZ\vvvert=\left\vvvert\begin{pmatrix}I_{r}-\cos\Theta\\
-\sin\Theta\end{pmatrix}\right\vvvert.

(94)

Note that the matrix on the right-hand side of (
94
) has singular values

\sqrt{(1-\cos\theta_{i})^{2}+\sin^{2}\theta_{i}}=2\sin\left(\frac{\theta_{i}}{2}\right)

for
i=1,\cdots,r
. Then by Theorem
21
,

\min_{O\in\mathbb{O}^{r\times r}}\vvvert UO-V\vvvert\leq f(2\sin(\theta_{1}/2),\cdots,2\sin\left(\theta_{r}/2)\right)

for the symmetric gauge function
f
associated with the norm. Combining the above fact with the inequality
\sin(\theta/2)=\frac{1}{2}\frac{\sin\theta}{\cos(\theta/2)}\leq\frac{\sin\theta}{\sqrt{2}}
for
\theta\in[0,\pi/2]
and Theorem
23
, we get the bound

\min_{O\in\mathbb{O}^{r\times r}}\vvvert UO-V\vvvert\leq f(\sqrt{2}\sin(\theta_{1}),\cdots,\sqrt{2}\sin(\theta_{r}))=\vvvert\sqrt{2}\sin\angle(U,V)\vvvert.

A.3.
Proof of Proposition
25

For any orthogonal matrix
O
, we first have

\displaystyle\|x^{\mathrm{T}}(V-UO)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|x^{\mathrm{T}}(V-P_{U}V)\|+\|x^{\mathrm{T}}(UU^{\mathrm{T}}V-UO)\|

\displaystyle=\|x^{\mathrm{T}}(V-P_{U}V)\|+\|x^{\mathrm{T}}U(U^{\mathrm{T}}V-O)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|x^{\mathrm{T}}(V-P_{U}V)\|+\|x^{\mathrm{T}}U\|\|U^{\mathrm{T}}V-O\|.

It remains to estimate
\|U^{\mathrm{T}}V-O\|
. Now consider a specific orthogonal matrix
O=O_{1}O_{2}^{\mathrm{T}}
, where as per (
28
), we have
U^{\mathrm{T}}V=O_{1}\cos\angle(U,V)O_{2}^{\mathrm{T}}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\|U^{\mathrm{T}}V-O\|

\displaystyle=\|O_{1}\cos\angle(U,V)O_{2}^{\mathrm{T}}-O_{1}O_{2}^{\mathrm{T}}\|=\|\cos\angle(U,V)-I_{r}\|

\displaystyle=1-\cos\theta_{r}\leq 1-\cos^{2}\theta_{r}=\sin^{2}\theta_{r}=\|\sin\angle(U,V)\|^{2}.

Putting these estimates together, we arrive at

\displaystyle\|x^{\mathrm{T}}(V-UO)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|x^{\mathrm{T}}(V-P_{U}V)\|+\|x^{\mathrm{T}}U\|\|\sin\angle(U,V)\|^{2}.

The other inequalities can be proved immediately by noting that

\displaystyle\left|x^{\mathrm{T}}(V-UO)y\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|x^{\mathrm{T}}(V-P_{U}V)y\right|+\left|x^{\mathrm{T}}(UU^{\mathrm{T}}V-UO)y\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|x^{\mathrm{T}}(V-P_{U}V)y\right|+\|x^{\mathrm{T}}U\|\|U^{\mathrm{T}}V-O\|

and

\displaystyle\|V-UO\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|V-P_{U}V\|_{2,\infty}+\|UU^{\mathrm{T}}V-UO\|_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\|V-P_{U}V\|_{2,\infty}+\|U\|_{2,\infty}\|U^{\mathrm{T}}V-O\|.

A.4.
Proof of (
15
)

As in (
28
), from the SVD of
{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{r}=O_{1}\cos\angle(U,\widetilde{U}_{r})O_{2}^{\mathrm{T}}
, we choose the orthogonal matrix
O=O_{1}O_{2}^{\mathrm{T}}
. For notational simplicity, let us denote
\cos\angle(U,\widetilde{U}_{r})=\diag(\cos\theta_{1},\cdots,\cos\theta_{r})=\cos\Theta
.

Using a similar argument as in the proof of Proposition
25
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{U}_{r}\widetilde{D}_{r}-{U}O\widetilde{D}_{r}\|_{2,\infty}\leq

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{U}_{r}\widetilde{D}_{r}-P_{U}\widetilde{U}_{r}\widetilde{D}_{r}\|_{2,\infty}+\|{U}\|_{2,\infty}\|({U}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{r}-O)\widetilde{D}_{r}\|.

(95)

It suffices to establish a bound for
\|({U}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{r}-O)\widetilde{D}_{r}\|
in the second term on the right-hand side of (
95
). Such a bound has been previously established in
[
79
, Lemma 15]
. To ensure our proof is self-contained, we repeat their proof here and provide the explicit constants.

Let
U_{0}
denote the matrix whose columns are orthonormal and span the complement of the subspace
U
. We first show that

\displaystyle O-{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{r}=2O_{1}\cdot\sin(\Theta/2)\cdot(\sin\Theta)^{-1}\sin(\Theta/2)\cdot O_{3}^{\mathrm{T}}U_{0}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{r}

(96)

for some orthogonal matrix
O_{3}
. Here
\sin(\Theta/2)=\diag(\sin(\theta_{1}/2),\cdots,\sin(\theta_{r}/2))
and

(\sin\Theta)^{-1}\sin(\Theta/2)=\diag\left(\frac{\sin(\theta_{1}/2)}{\sin(\theta_{1})},\cdots,\frac{\sin(\theta_{r}/2)}{\sin(\theta_{r})}\right).

To see (
96
), since

\displaystyle(\widetilde{U}_{r}^{\mathrm{T}}U_{0})(\widetilde{U}_{r}^{\mathrm{T}}U_{0})^{\mathrm{T}}=\widetilde{U}_{r}^{\mathrm{T}}U_{0}U_{0}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{r}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{U}_{r}^{\mathrm{T}}(I-UU^{\mathrm{T}})\widetilde{U}_{r}

\displaystyle=I-O_{2}\cos^{2}(\Theta)O_{2}^{\mathrm{T}}=O_{2}\sin^{2}(\Theta)O_{2}^{\mathrm{T}},

the SVD of
\widetilde{U}_{r}^{\mathrm{T}}U_{0}
is given by

\displaystyle\widetilde{U}_{r}^{\mathrm{T}}U_{0}=O_{2}(\sin\Theta)O_{3}^{\mathrm{T}}

(97)

for some orthogonal matrix
O_{3}
. Combining (
97
) with

O-{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{r}=O_{1}(I-\cos\Theta)O_{2}^{\mathrm{T}}=2O_{1}\sin^{2}(\Theta/2)O_{2}^{\mathrm{T}},

we prove (
96
) and consequently

\displaystyle(O-{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{r})\widetilde{D}_{r}=2O_{1}\cdot\sin(\Theta/2)\cdot(\sin\Theta)^{-1}\sin(\Theta/2)\cdot O_{3}^{\mathrm{T}}U_{0}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{r}\widetilde{D}_{r}

(98)

To bound
\|(O-{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{r})\widetilde{D}_{r}\|
, first observe

\displaystyle\|\sin(\Theta/2)\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\|\sin\Theta\|\quad\text{and}\quad\|(\sin\Theta)^{-1}\sin(\Theta/2)\|\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}

by the facts that
\cos(\theta/2)\geq 1/\sqrt{2}
and
\sin(\theta/2)=\frac{\sin\theta}{2\cos(\theta/2)}\leq\frac{\sqrt{2}}{2}\sin\theta
for
\theta\in[0,\pi/2]
. Then continuing from (
98
), we have

\displaystyle\|(O-{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{r})\widetilde{D}_{r}\|\leq\|\sin\Theta\|\cdot\|U_{0}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{r}\widetilde{D}_{r}\|.

It remains to bound
\|U_{0}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{r}\widetilde{D}_{r}\|
. Note that
\widetilde{U}_{r}\widetilde{D}_{r}=\widetilde{A}\widetilde{V}_{r}
. Multiplying
U_{0}^{\mathrm{T}}
on both sides and using
U_{0}^{\mathrm{T}}A=0
, we get the desired bound

\displaystyle\|U_{0}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{U}_{r}\widetilde{D}_{r}\|=\|U_{0}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{A}\widetilde{V}_{r}\|=\|U_{0}^{\mathrm{T}}(A+E)\widetilde{V}_{r}\|=\|U_{0}^{\mathrm{T}}E\widetilde{V}_{r}\|\leq\|E\|.

This completes the proof.

Appendix B
Proofs of Lemma
27
and Lemma
28

B.1.
Proof of Lemma
27

By the rotational invariance of
E
and definition of
\mathcal{E}
, we observe that for any orthogonal matrices

\mathcal{O}_{1}=\begin{pmatrix}O_{1}&0\\
0&O_{2}\end{pmatrix},\,\mathcal{O}_{2}=\begin{pmatrix}\widehat{O}_{1}&0\\
0&\widehat{O}_{2}\end{pmatrix}

where
O_{1},\widehat{O}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{N\times N}
and
O_{2},\widehat{O}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
are orthogonal matrices,

\mathcal{O}_{1}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{O}_{2}\sim\mathcal{E}.

Consequently,

\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}\left(G(z)-\Phi(z)\right)\mathbf{y}\sim(\mathcal{O}_{1}\mathbf{x})^{\mathrm{T}}\left(G(z)-\Phi(z)\right)(\mathcal{O}_{2}\mathbf{y}).

Hence, it suffices to assume
\mathbf{x}=(x_{1},0\cdots,0,x_{N+1},0,\cdots,0)^{\mathrm{T}}
with
x_{1}^{2}+x_{N+1}^{2}=1
and
\mathbf{y}=(y_{1},0\cdots,0,y_{N+1},0,\cdots,0)^{\mathrm{T}}
with
y_{1}^{2}+y_{N+1}^{2}=1
. Furthermore,

\displaystyle\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}\left(G(z)-\Phi(z)\right)\mathbf{y}

\displaystyle=x_{1}y_{1}(G_{11}(z)-\Phi_{11}(z))+x_{N+1}y_{N+1}(G_{N+1,N+1}(z)-\Phi_{N+1,N+1}(z))

\displaystyle\quad+x_{1}y_{N+1}G_{1,N+1}(z)+x_{N+1}y_{1}G_{N+1,1}(z).

(99)

In order to prove Lemma
27
, it suffices to show that for each fixed
k\in\llbracket 1,N+n\rrbracket
,

\displaystyle\left|G_{kk}(z)-\Phi_{kk}(z)\right|\leq\frac{2b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\frac{\sqrt{(K+1)\log(N+n)}}{|z|^{2}}

(100)

with probability at least
1-4(N+n)^{-(K+1)}
and for fixed
i\neq j\in\llbracket 1,N+n\rrbracket
,

\displaystyle|G_{ij}(z)|\leq 2\sqrt{2}\left(\frac{b}{b-1}\right)^{2}\frac{\sqrt{(K+1)\log(N+n)}}{|z|^{2}}

(101)

with probability at least
1-0.5(N+n)^{-(K+1)}
.

If so, continuing from (
B.1
), we find that

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}\left(G(z)-\Phi(z)\right)\mathbf{y}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left(2\left(\frac{b}{b-1}\right)^{2}+2\sqrt{2}\left(\frac{b}{b-1}\right)^{2}\right)\frac{\sqrt{(K+1)\log(N+n)}}{|z|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 5\left(\frac{b}{b-1}\right)^{2}\frac{\sqrt{(K+1)\log(N+n)}}{|z|^{2}}

with probability at least
1-9(N+n)^{-(K+1)}
. Here, we use the fact that
|x_{1}y_{1}|+|x_{N+1}y_{N+1}|\leq 1
by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.
The proofs of Equation (
100
) and Equation (
101
) closely resemble the proof presented in
[
64
, Lemma 28]
, with only minor cosmetic modifications. For the sake of brevity, we will omit the detailed proofs here.

B.2.
Proof of Lemma
28

In this section, we prove Lemma
28
using Lemma
27
and a standard
\varepsilon
-net argument.

For convenience, denote
\Delta(z):=G(z)-\Phi(z)
.
We first show that for any fixed
z\in\mathbb{C}
with
|z|\geq 2b(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})
,

\left\|\mathcal{U}^{T}\Delta(z)\mathcal{U}\right\|\leq 10\left(\frac{b}{b-1}\right)^{2}\frac{\sqrt{(K+1)\log(N+n)+2(\log 9)r}}{|z|^{2}}
