with probability at least
1-9(N+n)^{-(K+1)}
.

Let
\mathcal{N}
be the
{1}/{4}
-net of the unit sphere
\mathbb{S}^{2r-1}
. A simple volume argument (see for instance
[
70
, Corollary 4.2.13]
) shows
\mathcal{N}
can be chosen such that
|\mathcal{N}|\leq 9^{2r}
. Furthermore, since for any
\mathbf{y}\in\mathbb{S}^{2r-1}
, there exists a
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{N}
such that
\|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{x}\|\leq 1/4
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{y}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Delta(z)\mathcal{U}\mathbf{y}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Delta(z)\mathcal{U}\mathbf{x}\right|+\left|(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{x})^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Delta(z)\mathcal{U}\mathbf{x}\right|+\left|\mathbf{y}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Delta(z)\mathcal{U}(\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{x})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Delta(z)\mathcal{U}\mathbf{x}\right|+\frac{1}{2}\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Delta(z)\mathcal{U}\|.

Therefore,

\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Delta(z)\mathcal{U}\|\leq 2\max_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{N}}\left|\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Delta(z)\mathcal{U}\mathbf{x}\right|

and for any
K_{1}>0
and
(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})^{2}\geq 32(K_{1}+1)\log(N+n)
, by the union bound,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Delta(z)\mathcal{U}\|\geq 10\left(\frac{b}{b-1}\right)^{2}\frac{\sqrt{(K_{1}+1)\log(N+n)}}{|z|^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}\left(\max_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{N}}\left|\mathbf{x}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Delta(z)\mathcal{U}\mathbf{x}\right|\geq 5\left(\frac{b}{b-1}\right)^{2}\frac{\sqrt{(K_{1}+1)\log(N+n)}}{|z|^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 9^{2r+1}(N+n)^{-(K_{1}+1)},

where in the last inequality, we applied Lemma
27
. Now choose
K_{1}=K+\frac{2\log 9}{\log(N+n)}r
and assume

(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})^{2}\geq 32(K_{1}+1)\log(N+n)=32(K+1)\log(N+n)+64(\log 9)r.

The conclusion becomes that

\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Delta(z)\mathcal{U}\|\leq 10\left(\frac{b}{b-1}\right)^{2}\frac{\sqrt{(K+1)\log(N+n)+2(\log 9)r}}{|z|^{2}}

with probability at least
1-10(N+n)^{-(K+1)}
.

In particular, for any
z\in\mathsf{D}=\{z\in\mathbb{C}:2b(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})\leq|z|\leq 2n^{3}\}
,

\left\|\mathcal{U}^{T}\Delta(z)\mathcal{U}\right\|\leq 10\left(\frac{b}{b-1}\right)^{2}\frac{\sqrt{(K+7)\log(N+n)+2(\log 9)r}}{|z|^{2}}

with probability at least
1-9(N+n)^{-(K+7)}
, as long as

\displaystyle(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})^{2}\geq 32(K+7)\log(N+n)+64(\log 9)r.

(102)

Let
\mathcal{N}
be a
1
-net of
\mathsf{D}
. A simple volume argument (see for instance
[
65
, Lemma 3.3]
) shows
\mathcal{N}
can be chosen so that
|\mathcal{N}|\leq(1+8n^{3})^{2}<n^{7}
.
By the union bound,

\max_{z\in\mathcal{N}}|z|^{2}\left\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Delta(z)\mathcal{U}\right\|\leq 10\left(\frac{b}{b-1}\right)^{2}\sqrt{(K+7)\log(N+n)+2(\log 9)r}

(103)

with probability at least
1-9(N+n)^{-K}
. We now wish to extend this bound to all
z\in\mathsf{D}
.

Define the functions

f(z):=z^{2}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}G(z)\mathcal{U},\qquad g(z):=z^{2}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)\mathcal{U}.

In order to complete the proof, it suffices to show that
f
and
g
are
\frac{3b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}
-Lipschitz in
\mathsf{D}
. In other words, we want to show that
\|f(z)-f(w)\|\leq\frac{3b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}|z-w|
and
\|g(z)-g(w)\|\leq\frac{3b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}|z-w|
for all
z,w\in\mathsf{D}
. Indeed, in view of (
103
), if
z\in\mathsf{D}
, then there exists
w\in\mathcal{N}
so that
|z-w|\leq 1
, and hence

\displaystyle|z|^{2}\left\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}G(z)\mathcal{U}-\mathcal{U}^{T}\Phi(z)\mathcal{U}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\|f(z)-f(w)\|+\|f(w)-g(w)\|+\|g(w)-g(z)\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{6b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}+|w|^{2}\left\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}G(w)\mathcal{U}-\mathcal{U}^{T}\Phi(w)\mathcal{U}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{6b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}+10\left(\frac{b}{b-1}\right)^{2}\sqrt{(K+7)\log(N+n)+2(\log 9)r}

\displaystyle<11\left(\frac{b}{b-1}\right)^{2}\sqrt{(K+7)\log(N+n)+2(\log 9)r}=\eta,

where we used the Lipschitz continuity of
f
and
g
in the second inequality. In the last inequality, we use (
102
) to obtain a crude bound
N+n\geq 32\cdot 7\log(N+n)
and hence
N+n\geq 1600
. This implies
\sqrt{(K+7)\log(N+n)+2(\log 9)r}\geq\sqrt{7\log(1600)+2(\log 9)}\approx 7.5
.

It remains to show that
f
and
g
are
\frac{3b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}
-Lipschitz in
\mathsf{D}
. Recall that we work on the event where
\|E\|\leq 2(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})
through the proofs. Let
z,w\in\mathsf{D}
, and assume without loss of generality that
|z|\geq|w|\geq 2b(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})
. Then

\displaystyle\|f(z)-f(w)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|z^{2}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}G(z)\mathcal{U}-zw\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}G(z)\mathcal{U}\|+\|zw\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}G(z)\mathcal{U}-w^{2}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}G(z)\mathcal{U}\|

\displaystyle\qquad+\|w^{2}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}G(z)\mathcal{U}-w^{2}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}G(w)\mathcal{U}\|

\displaystyle\leq|z|\|G(z)\||z-w|+|w||z-w|\|G(z)\|+|w|^{2}|z-w|\|G(z)\|\|G(w)\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2b}{b-1}|z-w|+\frac{b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}|z-w|=\frac{b(3b-2)}{(b-1)^{2}}|z-w|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}|z-w|,

where we used the resolvent identity
B^{-1}-C^{-1}=B^{-1}(C-B)C^{-1}
, Lemma
26
, and the fact that
\frac{|w|}{|z|}\leq 1
. This shows that
f
is
\frac{3b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}
-Lipschitz in
\mathsf{D}
.

The proof for
g
is similar. First, by the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|g(z)

\displaystyle-g(w)\|

\displaystyle\leq\|z^{2}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)\mathcal{U}-zw\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)U\|+\|zw\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)\mathcal{U}-w^{2}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)\mathcal{U}\|

\displaystyle\qquad+\|w^{2}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)\mathcal{U}-w^{2}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(w)\mathcal{U}\|

\displaystyle\leq|z||z-w|\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)\mathcal{U}\|+|w||z-w|\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)\mathcal{U}\|+|w|^{2}\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}(\Phi(z)-\Phi(w))\mathcal{U}\|.

(104)

Using the explicit expression in (
34
), we find that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}(\Phi(z)-\Phi(w))\mathcal{U}\|=\max\left\{\frac{|\phi_{1}(z)-\phi_{1}(w)|}{|\phi_{1}(z)\phi_{1}(w)|},\frac{|\phi_{2}(z)-\phi_{2}(w)|}{|\phi_{2}(z)\phi_{2}(w)|}\right\}.

By (
32
) and the resolvent identity
B^{-1}-C^{-1}=B^{-1}(C-B)C^{-1}
,

\displaystyle|\phi_{1}(z)-\phi_{1}(w)|

\displaystyle=|z-w-\tr\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{d}}(G(z)-G(w))|

\displaystyle=|z-w-(z-w)\tr\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{d}}G(z)G(w)|

\displaystyle\leq|z-w|\left(1+(N+n)\|G(z)\|\|G(w)\|\right)

\displaystyle\leq|z-w|\left(1+\frac{b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\frac{(N+n)}{|z||w|}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\frac{1}{4(b-1)^{2}}\right)|z-w|,

where we used Lemma
26
and the facts that
\frac{N+n}{|w|}\leq\frac{|w|}{4b^{2}}
and
\frac{|w|}{|z|}\leq 1
. The same upper bound also holds for
|\phi_{2}(z)-\phi_{2}(w)|
. Combining these estimates with (
36
), we have

\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}(\Phi(z)-\Phi(w))\mathcal{U}\|\leq\frac{1+1/4(b-1)^{2}}{(1-1/4b(b-1))^{2}}\frac{|z-w|}{|z||w|}.

Notice that

\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)\mathcal{U}\|\leq\frac{1}{1-1/4b(b-1)}\frac{1}{|z|}
for any
|z|\geq 2b(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})
, which can be verified using (
35
) and the bounds in (
36
). Inserting these bounds into (
B.2
) yields that

\displaystyle\|g(z)-g(w)\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{1-1/4b(b-1)}|z-w|+\frac{1+1/4(b-1)^{2}}{(1-1/4b(b-1))^{2}}|z-w|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4b(12b^{3}-24b^{2}+11b+2)}{(4b^{2}-4b-1)^{2}}|z-w|<\frac{3b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}|z-w|,

where the last inequality is check via Mathematica. Hence,
g
is
\frac{3b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}
-Lipschitz in
\mathsf{D}
.

Appendix C
Proof of Lemma
52

In this section, we estimate
\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|
for each
i\in I=\llbracket k,s\rrbracket\cup\llbracket r+k,r+s\rrbracket
. Recall the decomposition of
\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}
in (
45
):

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}=\Pi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}+\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i},

(105)

where
\Pi(z)
is a function to be further specified during the proof, and
\Xi(z)=G(z)-\Pi(z)
.

We split the estimation of
\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|
for
i\in I
into two cases: when
|\lambda_{i}|
is large and when
|\lambda_{i}|
is relatively small.

We start with the simpler case when
|\lambda_{i}|>n^{2}/2
. We choose

\Pi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}):=\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}}I_{N+n}+\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}^{2}}\mathcal{E}.

We work on the event

\max_{l\in\llbracket 1,r_{0}\rrbracket:\sigma_{l}>\frac{1}{2}n^{2}}|\widetilde{\sigma}_{l}-\sigma_{l}|\leq\eta r.

By Lemma
31
, this event holds with probability at least
1-(N+n)^{-1.5r^{2}(K+4)}
. Hence,
|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|\geq|\lambda_{i}|-\eta r\geq 2b(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})
. We apply Lemma
30
to get

\left\|\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\right\|=\left\|G(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})-\Pi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\right\|\leq\frac{b}{b-1}\frac{\|\mathcal{E}\|^{2}}{|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|^{3}}.

(106)

Multiplying
\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}
on both sides of (
105
), we obtain the following equation:

\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}=\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\Pi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}+\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}.

Plugging in (
46
) and using the facts
\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{U}_{I}=0
and
\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{U}_{J}=I
, we further get

\displaystyle\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}=\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}}\mathcal{D}_{J}\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}+\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}^{2}}\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{U}\mathcal{D}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}+\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\mathcal{D}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i},

which, by rearranging the terms, is reduced to

\displaystyle(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}I-\mathcal{D}_{J})\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}=\frac{1}{\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}}\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{U}\mathcal{D}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}+\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}\mathcal{D}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\min_{j\in J}|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}-\lambda_{j}|\cdot\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|\leq\frac{1}{|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|}\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{U}\|\cdot\|\mathcal{D}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|+|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|\|\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\|\cdot\|\mathcal{D}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|.

Note that
\|\mathcal{D}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|\leq\|\mathcal{E}\|+|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|\leq\frac{b}{b-1}|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|
as in (
62
). Inserting (
106
) into the above inequality, we arrive at

\displaystyle\min_{j\in J}|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}-\lambda_{j}|\cdot\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|\leq\frac{b}{b-1}\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{U}\|+\frac{b^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\frac{\|E\|^{2}}{|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|}.

(107)

For the remaining arguments, we work on the event

\displaystyle\mathsf{F}:=\big\{\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{U}\|\leq 2\sqrt{r}+\sqrt{2(K+7)\log(N+n)}\big\}.

The following lemma is proved in
[
63
, Lemma 18]
.

Lemma 35
.

Let
K
be an arbitrary positive constant. With probability at least
1-2(N+n)^{-K}
, we have

\|\mathcal{U}^{T}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{U}\|\leq 2\sqrt{r}+\sqrt{2K\log(N+n)}.

Therefore, the event
\mathsf{F}
holds with probability at least
1-2(N+n)^{-(K+7)}.
We continue the estimation of
\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|
from (
107
). Note that

\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{E}\mathcal{U}\|\leq 2\sqrt{r}+\sqrt{2(K+7)\log(N+n)}<\eta.

Also,
{\|E\|^{2}}/{|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|}\leq 4(2\sqrt{n})^{2}/n^{2}<\eta
where we used the crude bound
|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}|\geq\frac{1}{4}n^{2}
by Weyl’s inequality. It follows that

\min_{j\in J}|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}-\lambda_{j}|\cdot\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|\leq\frac{b(2b-1)}{(b-1)^{2}}\eta.

(108)

To bound the left-hand side of (
108
), we first consider
i\in\llbracket k,s\rrbracket
. Then

\min_{j\in J}|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}-\lambda_{j}|=\min_{j\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket\cup\llbracket s+1,r\rrbracket}|\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}-\sigma_{j}|=\min\{\sigma_{k-1}-\widetilde{\sigma}_{i},\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}-\sigma_{s+1}\}

by
|\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}-\sigma_{i}|\leq\eta r
and the supposition
\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}\geq 75\chi(b)\eta r
. Next, applying
\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}\geq 75\chi(b)\eta r
again, we get

\min_{j\in J}|\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}-\lambda_{j}|\geq\left(1-\frac{1}{75\chi(b)}\right)\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}.

It follows from (
108
) that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta}{\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}}\left(1-\frac{1}{75\chi(b)}\right)^{-1}\frac{b(2b-1)}{(b-1)^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{75(2b-1)^{3}b}{(b-1)^{2}(296b^{2}-296b+75)}\frac{\eta}{\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}}

\displaystyle<3\frac{(b+1)^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\frac{\eta}{\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}}

(109)

for every
i\in\llbracket k,s\rrbracket
satisfying
\lambda_{i}=\sigma_{i}\geq n^{2}/2
. The last inequality was checked by Mathematica. Finally, for
i\in\llbracket r+k,r+s\rrbracket
such that
|\lambda_{i}|\geq n^{2}/2
, analogous arguments yield that the same bound

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{U}}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|\leq 3\frac{(b+1)^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\frac{\eta}{\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}}.

(110)

The estimation of
\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|
when
|\lambda_{i}|
is relatively small is more involved. From the previous discussion, it suffices to assume there is a certain
l_{0}\in\llbracket 1,r_{0}\rrbracket
for which
\sigma_{l_{0}}\leq n^{2}/2
. We claim that there exists an index
i_{0}\in\llbracket 1,r_{0}\rrbracket
such that
\sigma_{j}\leq n^{3}
for
j\geq i_{0}
and
\sigma_{j}>n^{3}
for
j<i_{0}
, and

\delta_{i_{0}-1}=\sigma_{i_{0}-1}-\sigma_{i_{0}}\geq 75\chi(b)\eta r.

To determine
i_{0}
, we propose a simple iterative algorithm: start with
\sigma_{1}
. If
\sigma_{1}\leq n^{3}
, set
i_{0}=1
and terminate the algorithm, since
\sigma_{0}=\infty
and
\delta_{0}=\infty
by definition. Assume
\sigma_{1}>n^{3}
and evaluate
\sigma_{2}
. If
\sigma_{2}\leq n^{3}-75\chi(b)\eta r
, set
i_{0}=2
and exit. Assume
\sigma_{2}>n^{3}-75\chi(b)\eta r
and evaluate
\sigma_{3}
. We continue this process and terminate the algorithm with
i_{0}=k
unless

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}>n^{3},\sigma_{2}>n^{3}-75\chi(b)\eta r,\cdots,\sigma_{k}>n^{3}-75\chi(b)\eta r.

(111)

Note that the condition (
111
) cannot hold for
k=l_{0}
because
\sigma_{l_{0}}\leq n^{2}/2<n^{3}-75\chi(b)\eta r
, based on the assumption that
(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})^{2}\geq 32(K+7)\log(N+n)+64(\log 9)r
. Therefore,
i_{0}
must satisfy
i_{0}\leq l_{0}-1
.

We shall fix such an index
i_{0}
throughout the rest of the proof. We now turn our attention to estimating
\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|
for
i\in\llbracket k_{0},s\rrbracket\cup\llbracket r+k_{0},r+s\rrbracket
, where we define

k_{0}:=\max\{k,i_{0}\}

for the sake of notational simplicity. Note that
\min\{\delta_{k_{0}-1},\delta_{s}\}\geq 75\chi(b)\eta r
. Furthermore, in this scenario,
|\lambda_{i}|\leq n^{3}
. We take

\Pi(\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i})=\Phi(\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}).

Continuing from (
45
), we have

\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}=\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}+\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}

and multiply on the left by
\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}
to get

\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}=\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}+\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{A}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}.

(112)

Plugging in (
46
), we further have

\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}=\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}){\mathcal{U}}_{J}{\mathcal{D}}_{J}{\mathcal{U}}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}+\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}){\mathcal{U}}{\mathcal{D}}{\mathcal{U}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i},

where we used
\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\mathcal{U}_{I}=0
. Hence,

\left(I_{2(r-s+k-1)}-\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}){\mathcal{U}}_{J}{\mathcal{D}}_{J}\right)\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}=\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}){\mathcal{U}}{\mathcal{D}}{\mathcal{U}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}.

(113)

We are now in position to bound
\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|
. This can be achieved by obtaining an upper bound for the right-hand side of (
113
) and estimating the smallest singular value of the matrix

I_{2(r-s+k-1)}-\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}){\mathcal{U}}_{J}{\mathcal{D}}_{J}

(114)

on the left-hand side of (
113
). We establish these estimates in the following two steps. Recall that

\xi(b)=1+\frac{1}{2(b-1)^{2}}\quad\text{and}\quad\chi(b)=1+\frac{1}{4b(b-1)}.

For each
k_{0}\leq i\leq s
, by Theorem
29
, there exists
k_{0}\leq l_{i}\leq s
such that
\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}\in S_{\sigma_{l_{i}}}
specified in (
40
), and

\displaystyle|\varphi(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})-\sigma_{l_{i}}^{2}|\leq 20\xi(b)\chi(b)\eta r\left(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}+\chi(b)\sigma_{l_{i}}\right)

(115)

with probability at least
1-10(N+n)^{-K}
. Denote this event as
\mathsf{E}_{1}
. Furthermore, on the event
\mathsf{E}_{1}
, by Lemma
28
, for every
k_{0}\leq i\leq s
,

\left\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\mathcal{U}\right\|\leq\frac{\eta}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}^{2}}

holds with probability at least
1-9(N+n)^{-K}
. Let us denote this event as
\mathsf{E}_{2}
.

In the remaining proof, we will work on the event
\mathsf{E}_{1}\cap\mathsf{E}_{2}
which holds with probability at least
1-19(N+n)^{-K}
.

Step 1. Upper bound for the right-hand side of (
113
)
. We first consider the case when
i\in\llbracket k_{0},s\rrbracket
and
\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}=\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}
. Note that
\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}){\mathcal{U}}
is a sub-matrix of
\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}){\mathcal{U}}
. Thus, using (
6.2
) and the fact that the spectral norm of any sub-matrix is bounded by the spectral norm of the full matrix, we deduce that

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}){\mathcal{U}}\cdot{\mathcal{D}}{\mathcal{U}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\eta}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}^{2}}\|{\mathcal{D}}{\mathcal{U}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|.

Recall the bound in (
62
):

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{D}}{\mathcal{U}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|\leq\frac{b}{b-1}\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}

(116)

Hence,

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}){\mathcal{U}}{\mathcal{D}}{\mathcal{U}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{b}{b-1}\frac{\eta}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}}.

(117)

For the case when
i\in\llbracket r+k_{0},r+s\rrbracket
,
\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}=-\widetilde{\sigma}_{i-r}
. Observe that

G(-\widetilde{\sigma}_{i-r})=(-\widetilde{\sigma}_{i-r}-\mathcal{E})^{-1}=-(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i-r}+\mathcal{E})^{-1}\sim-(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i-r}-\mathcal{E})^{-1}=-G(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i-r})

because the distribution of
\mathcal{E}
is symmetric. Hence

\Phi(-\widetilde{\sigma}_{i-r})\sim-\Phi(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i-r})

by the definition (
30
). Repeating the arguments from the previous case, we see that

\displaystyle\left\|\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\Xi(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}){\mathcal{U}}{\mathcal{D}}{\mathcal{U}}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{b}{b-1}\frac{\eta}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i-r}}.

(118)

Step 2. Lower bound for the smallest singular value of the matrix (
114
)
.
In fact, the singular values of the matrix (
114
) can be calculated explicitly via elementary linear algebra. The following proposition presents a subtle modification of the one found in
[
63
, Proposition 10]
.

Proposition 36
.

For
1\leq r_{0}<r
and
1\leq k\leq s\leq r_{0}
, denote the index sets
I:=\llbracket k,s\rrbracket\cup\llbracket r+k,r+s\rrbracket
and
J:=\llbracket 1,2r\rrbracket\setminus I
. For any
x\in\mathbb{R}
satisfying
|x|>\|\mathcal{E}\|
, the singular values of
I_{2(r-s+k-1)}-\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(x){\mathcal{U}}_{J}\mathcal{D}_{J}
are given by

\displaystyle\left|\sqrt{1+\beta(x)^{2}\sigma_{t}^{2}}\pm|\alpha(x)|\sigma_{t}\right|

for
t\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket\cup\llbracket s+1,r\rrbracket
.

In order to bound the singular values, we first estimate
\phi_{1}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\phi_{2}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})
,
\phi_{1}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})
and
\phi_{2}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})
for
i\in\llbracket k_{0},s\rrbracket
. Since
\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}\in S_{\sigma_{l_{i}}}
for some
l_{i}\in\llbracket k_{0},s\rrbracket
where
S_{\sigma}
is defined in (
40
), we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}\geq\sigma_{l_{i}}-20\chi(b)\eta r\geq\left(1-\frac{\chi(b)}{4b}\right)\sigma_{l_{i}}

(119)

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}\leq\chi(b)\sigma_{l_{i}}+20\chi(b)\eta r\leq\chi(b)\left(1+\frac{1}{4b}\right)\sigma_{l_{i}}

(120)

by the supposition
\sigma_{l_{i}}\geq 2b(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})+80b\eta r
.

Observe from (
36
) that

\left(1-\frac{1}{4b(b-1)}\right)\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}\leq\phi_{s}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\leq\chi(b)\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}\quad\text{for }s=1,2.

(121)

Using these estimates, we crudely bound

0<\alpha(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{\phi_{1}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})}+\frac{1}{\phi_{2}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})}\right)\leq\frac{\tau(b)}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}}\quad\text{with }\tau(b):=\left(1-\frac{1}{4b(b-1)}\right)^{-1}

and by (
33
),

\displaystyle\beta(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{1}{\phi_{1}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})}-\frac{1}{\phi_{2}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})}\right)=\frac{\phi_{2}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})-\phi_{1}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})}{2\phi_{1}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\phi_{2}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})}

\displaystyle=\frac{n-N}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}}\frac{1}{2\phi_{1}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\phi_{2}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})}\leq\frac{\tau(b)^{2}}{8b^{2}\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}}

by noting that
\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}^{2}\geq(2b(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n}))^{2}>4b^{2}(N+n)
.

We are ready to bound the singular values of
I_{2(r-s+k-1)}-\mathcal{U}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}){\mathcal{U}}_{J}{\mathcal{D}}_{J}
. We start with the case when
i\in\llbracket k_{0},s\rrbracket
and
\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}=\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}
. In view of Proposition
36
, the goal is to bound

\displaystyle\min_{t\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket\cup\llbracket s+1,r\rrbracket}\left|\sqrt{1+\beta(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})^{2}\sigma_{t}^{2}}\pm|\alpha(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})|\sigma_{t}\right|

\displaystyle=\min_{t\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket\cup\llbracket s+1,r\rrbracket}\left|\sqrt{1+\beta(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})^{2}\sigma_{t}^{2}}-\alpha(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\sigma_{t}\right|

\displaystyle=\min_{t\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket\cup\llbracket s+1,r\rrbracket}\left|\frac{1-(\alpha(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})^{2}-\beta(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})^{2})\sigma_{t}^{2}}{\sqrt{1+\beta(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})^{2}\sigma_{t}^{2}}+\alpha(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\sigma_{t}}\right|

\displaystyle=\min_{t\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket\cup\llbracket s+1,r\rrbracket}\frac{\left|1-\frac{\sigma_{t}^{2}}{\phi_{1}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\phi_{2}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})}\right|}{\sqrt{1+\beta(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})^{2}\sigma_{t}^{2}}+\alpha(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\sigma_{t}}.

The upper bounds of
\alpha(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})
and
\beta(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})
obtained above yield that

\displaystyle\sqrt{1+\beta(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})^{2}\sigma_{t}^{2}}+\alpha(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\sigma_{t}\leq\sqrt{1+\frac{\tau(b)^{4}}{64b^{4}}\frac{\sigma_{t}^{2}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}^{2}}}+\tau(b)\frac{\sigma_{t}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}}\leq 1+\tau(b)\left(1+\frac{\tau(b)}{8b^{2}}\right)\frac{\sigma_{t}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}}

for any
t\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket\cup\llbracket s+1,r\rrbracket
. Hence,

\displaystyle\min_{t\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket\cup\llbracket s+1,r\rrbracket}\left|\sqrt{1+\beta(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})^{2}\sigma_{t}^{2}}\pm\alpha(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\sigma_{t}\right|

\displaystyle\geq\min_{t\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket\cup\llbracket s+1,r\rrbracket}\frac{1}{1+\tau(b)\left(1+\frac{\tau(b)}{8b^{2}}\right)\frac{\sigma_{t}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}}}\left|\frac{\phi_{1}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\phi_{2}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})-\sigma_{t}^{2}}{\phi_{1}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\phi_{2}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})}\right|

\displaystyle\geq\min_{t\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket\cup\llbracket s+1,r\rrbracket}\frac{1}{1+\tau(b)\left(1+\frac{\tau(b)}{8b^{2}}\right)\frac{\sigma_{t}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}}}\frac{|\phi_{1}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\phi_{2}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})-\sigma_{t}^{2}|}{\chi(b)^{2}\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}^{2}}.

To continue the estimates, we consider the cases
t\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket
and
t\in\llbracket s+1,r\rrbracket
separately.

First, for any
t\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket
,
\sigma_{t}\geq\sigma_{i}
and
\sigma_{t}\geq\sigma_{l_{i}}
since
i,l_{i}\in\llbracket k_{0},s\rrbracket
. By (
115
),

\phi_{1}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\phi_{2}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\leq\sigma_{l_{i}}^{2}+20\xi(b)\chi(b)\eta r(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}+\chi(b)\sigma_{l_{i}}).

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\sigma_{t}^{2}-\phi_{1}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\phi_{2}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})

\displaystyle\geq\sigma_{t}^{2}-\sigma_{l_{i}}^{2}-20\xi(b)\chi(b)\eta r(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}+\chi(b)\sigma_{l_{i}})

\displaystyle\geq(\sigma_{t}-\sigma_{l_{i}})(\sigma_{t}+\sigma_{l_{i}})-20\xi(b)\chi(b)^{2}\eta r\left(\big(1+\frac{1}{4b}\big)\sigma_{t}+\sigma_{l_{i}}\right).

(122)

The last inequality is due to

\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}\leq\chi(b)\left(1+\frac{1}{4b}\right)\sigma_{l_{i}}\leq\chi(b)\left(1+\frac{1}{4b}\right)\sigma_{t}

from (
120
) and
\sigma_{t}\geq\sigma_{l_{i}}
. Since
\sigma_{t}-\sigma_{l_{i}}\geq\delta_{k-1}\geq 75\chi(b)\eta r
, we further get

\displaystyle\sigma_{t}^{2}-\phi_{1}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\phi_{2}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\frac{4}{15}\xi(b)\chi(b)\big(1+\frac{1}{4b}\big)\right)\delta_{k-1}(\sigma_{t}+\sigma_{l_{i}})>0

since
1-\frac{4}{15}\xi(b)\chi(b)\big(1+\frac{1}{4b}\big)\geq 1-\frac{4}{15}\xi(2)\chi(2)\big(1+\frac{1}{8}\big)\approx 0.49
.

Hence, we further have

\displaystyle\min_{t\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket}\frac{1}{1+\tau(b)\left(1+\frac{\tau(b)}{8b^{2}}\right)\frac{\sigma_{t}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}}}\frac{|\phi_{1}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\phi_{2}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})-\sigma_{t}^{2}|}{\chi(b)^{2}\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\chi(b)^{2}\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}}\min_{t\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket}\frac{\sigma_{t}^{2}-\phi_{1}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\phi_{2}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}+\tau(b)\left(1+\frac{\tau(b)}{8b^{2}}\right){\sigma_{t}}}

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\frac{4}{15}\xi(b)\chi(b)\big(1+\frac{1}{4b}\big)\right)\frac{\delta_{k-1}}{\chi(b)^{2}\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}}\min_{t\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket}\frac{\sigma_{t}+\sigma_{l_{i}}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}+\tau(b)\left(1+\frac{\tau(b)}{8b^{2}}\right){\sigma_{t}}}

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\frac{4}{15}\xi(b)\chi(b)\big(1+\frac{1}{4b}\big)\right)\frac{\delta_{k-1}}{\chi(b)^{2}\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}}\min_{t\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket}\frac{\sigma_{l_{i}}+\sigma_{t}}{\chi(b)\left(1+\frac{1}{4b}\right)\sigma_{l_{i}}+\tau(b)\left(1+\frac{\tau(b)}{8b^{2}}\right){\sigma_{t}}}.

Note that
\chi(b)\left(1+\frac{1}{4b}\right)\geq\tau(b)\left(1+\frac{\tau(b)}{8b^{2}}\right)
for
b\geq 2
(checked via Mathematics). We conclude that

\displaystyle\min_{t\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket}\left|\sqrt{1+\beta(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})^{2}\sigma_{t}^{2}}\pm\alpha(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\sigma_{t}\right|\geq\frac{1-\frac{4}{15}\xi(b)\chi(b)\big(1+\frac{1}{4b}\big)}{\chi(b)^{3}\left(1+\frac{1}{4b}\right)}\frac{\delta_{k-1}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}}.

(123)

Next, for any
t\in\llbracket s+1,r\rrbracket
,
\sigma_{t}/\sigma_{l_{i}}\leq 1
and by (
119
),
\sigma_{t}/{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}}\leq\left(1-\frac{\chi(b)}{4b}\right)^{-1}.
Consequently,

\displaystyle\min_{t\in\llbracket s+1,r\rrbracket}\frac{1}{1+\tau(b)\left(1+\frac{\tau(b)}{8b^{2}}\right)\frac{\sigma_{t}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}}}\frac{|\phi_{1}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\phi_{2}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})-\sigma_{t}^{2}|}{\chi(b)^{2}\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}^{2}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{1+\tau(b)\left(1+\frac{\tau(b)}{8b^{2}}\right)\left(1-\frac{\chi(b)}{4b}\right)^{-1}}\frac{1}{\chi(b)^{2}}\min_{t\in\llbracket s+1,r\rrbracket}\frac{|\phi_{1}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\phi_{2}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})-\sigma_{t}^{2}|}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}^{2}}.

(124)

By (
115
),

\phi_{1}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\phi_{2}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\geq\sigma_{l_{i}}^{2}-20\xi(b)\chi(b)\eta r(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}+\chi(b)\sigma_{l_{i}}).

Using a similar argument as (
C
), one has

\displaystyle\phi_{1}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\phi_{2}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})-\sigma_{t}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\sigma_{l_{i}}^{2}-\sigma_{t}^{2}-20\xi(b)\chi(b)^{2}\eta r\left(\big(1+\frac{1}{4b}\big)\sigma_{t}+\sigma_{l_{i}}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\frac{4}{15}\xi(b)\chi(b)\big(1+\frac{1}{4b}\big)\right)\delta_{s}(\sigma_{t}+\sigma_{l_{i}})>0

since
\sigma_{l_{i}}-\sigma_{t}\geq\delta_{s}\geq 75\chi(b)\eta r
. Continuing from (
C
), we further get

\displaystyle\min_{t\in\llbracket s+1,r\rrbracket}\left|\sqrt{1+\beta(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})^{2}\sigma_{t}^{2}}\pm\alpha(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i})\sigma_{t}\right|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1-\frac{4}{15}\xi(b)\chi(b)\big(1+\frac{1}{4b}\big)}{1+\tau(b)\left(1+\frac{\tau(b)}{8b^{2}}\right)\left(1-\frac{\chi(b)}{4b}\right)^{-1}}\frac{1}{\chi(b)^{2}}\frac{\delta_{s}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}}\min_{t\in\llbracket s+1,r\rrbracket}\frac{\sigma_{t}+\sigma_{l_{i}}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1-\frac{4}{15}\xi(b)\chi(b)\big(1+\frac{1}{4b}\big)}{1+\tau(b)\left(1+\frac{\tau(b)}{8b^{2}}\right)\left(1-\frac{\chi(b)}{4b}\right)^{-1}}\frac{1}{\chi(b)^{3}\big(1+\frac{1}{4b}\big)}\frac{\delta_{s}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}}:=\nu(b)\frac{\delta_{s}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}},

where the last inequality follows from (
120
).

Comparing (
123
) and (
125
), together with the observation

\frac{1-\frac{4}{15}\xi(b)\chi(b)\big(1+\frac{1}{4b}\big)}{\chi(b)^{3}\left(1+\frac{1}{4b}\right)}\geq\nu(b)

for
b\geq 2
(checked via Mathematica), we conclude that

\displaystyle\min_{t\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket\cup\llbracket s+1,r\rrbracket}\left|\sqrt{1+\beta(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})^{2}\sigma_{t}^{2}}\pm|\alpha(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})|\sigma_{t}\right|\geq\nu(b)\frac{\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i}}.

(125)

For the case when
i\in\llbracket r+k_{0},r+s\rrbracket
and
\widetilde{\lambda}_{i}=-\widetilde{\sigma}_{i-r}
. Use the observation that
\alpha(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\sim-\alpha(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i-r})
and
\beta(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})\sim-\beta(\widetilde{\sigma}_{i-r})
from the definitions (
32
). A simple modification of the previous proof shows that

\displaystyle\min_{t\in\llbracket 1,k-1\rrbracket\cup\llbracket s+1,r\rrbracket}\left|\sqrt{1+\beta(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})^{2}\sigma_{t}^{2}}\pm|\alpha(\widetilde{\lambda}_{i})|\sigma_{t}\right|\geq\nu(b)\frac{\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{i-r}}.

(126)

Step 3. Combining the bounds above
.
With the estimates deduced in the previous two steps, we are in a position to bound
\|{\mathcal{U}}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|
. For
i\in\llbracket k_{0},s\rrbracket
,
plugging (
117
) and (
125
) and into (
113
), we find that

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{U}}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{b}{(b-1)\nu(b)}\frac{\eta}{\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}}.

Finally, for simplicity, we employ the following bound

\frac{b}{(b-1)\nu(b)}<3\frac{(b+1)^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}

for
b\geq 2
(checked via Mathematica). We arrive at

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{U}}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|

\displaystyle\leq 3\frac{(b+1)^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\frac{\eta}{\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}}.

Likewise, for
i\in\llbracket r+k_{0},r+s\rrbracket
, using (
118
) and (
126
), we also get

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{U}}_{J}^{\mathrm{T}}\widetilde{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|\leq 3\frac{(b+1)^{2}}{(b-1)^{2}}\frac{\eta}{\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}}.

This completes the proof.

Appendix D
Proof of Theorem
29

This section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
29
.
For convenience,
denote

M:=2b(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n}).

Note that the assumptions of Theorem
29
guarantees that for any
z\in S_{\sigma_{j}}
(
1\leq j\leq r_{0}
),
|z|\geq\operatorname{Re}(z)\geq\sigma_{j}-20\chi(b)\eta r\geq\sigma_{j}-20\chi(2)\eta r=\sigma_{j}-\frac{45}{2}\eta r>M
.

We start with some reduction of the proof. First, note that if
\sigma_{j}>n^{2}/2
for
1\leq j\leq r_{0}
, then by Lemma
31
, with probability at least
1-(N+n)^{-1.5r^{2}(K+4)}
,

|\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}-\sigma_{j}|\leq\eta r.

Since
\varphi(z)=(z-\tr\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{d}}G(z))(z-\tr\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{u}}G(z))
,

\displaystyle|\varphi(\widetilde{\sigma}_{j})-\sigma_{j}^{2}|

\displaystyle=\left|\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}-\sigma_{j}^{2}-\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}\tr G(\widetilde{\sigma}_{j})+(\tr\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{u}}G(\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}))(\tr\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{d}}G(\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}))\right|

\displaystyle\leq\eta r(\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}+\sigma_{j})+\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}\left|\tr G(\widetilde{\sigma}_{j})\right|+\left|\tr\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{u}}G(\widetilde{\sigma}_{j})\right|\left|\tr\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{d}}G(\widetilde{\sigma}_{j})\right|.

Note that by Weyl’s inequality,
\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}\geq\sigma_{j}-\|E\|\geq\max\{M+80b\eta r,n^{2}/2\}-2(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})\geq M
by the suppositions on
N,n
. Hence, by (
37
),

\max\{|\tr G(\widetilde{\sigma}_{j})|,|\tr\mathcal{I}^{u}G(\widetilde{\sigma}_{j})|,|\tr\mathcal{I}^{d}G(\widetilde{\sigma}_{j})|\}\leq\frac{b}{b-1}\frac{N+n}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}}\leq 2\frac{N+n}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}}.

It follows that

\displaystyle|\varphi(\widetilde{\sigma}_{j})-\sigma_{j}^{2}|\leq\eta r(\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}+\sigma_{j})+2(N+n)+4\frac{(N+n)^{2}}{\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}^{2}}\leq 10\eta r(\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}+\sigma_{j})

by the supposition that
\sigma_{j}>n^{2}/2
and the Weyl’s inequality. In particular, the conclusion of Theorem
29
holds.

Consequently, it is enough to examine the scenario where there is a certain
l_{0}\in\llbracket 1,r_{0}\rrbracket
for which
\sigma_{l_{0}}\leq n^{2}/2
. We claim that there exists an index
i_{0}\in\llbracket 1,r_{0}\rrbracket
such that
\sigma_{j}\leq n^{3}
for
j\geq i_{0}
and
\sigma_{j}>n^{3}
for
j<i_{0}
, and

\delta_{i_{0}-1}=\sigma_{i_{0}-1}-\sigma_{i_{0}}\geq 75\chi(b)\eta r.

To determine
i_{0}
, we propose a simple iterative algorithm: start with
\sigma_{1}
. If
\sigma_{1}\leq n^{3}
, set
i_{0}=1
and terminate the algorithm, since
\sigma_{0}=\infty
and
\delta_{0}=\infty
by definition. Assume
\sigma_{1}>n^{3}
and evaluate
\sigma_{2}
. If
\sigma_{2}\leq n^{3}-75\chi(b)\eta r
, set
i_{0}=2
and exit. Assume
\sigma_{2}>n^{3}-75\chi(b)\eta r
and evaluate
\sigma_{3}
. We continue this process and terminate the algorithm with
i_{0}=k
unless

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}>n^{3},\sigma_{2}>n^{3}-75\chi(b)\eta r,\cdots,\sigma_{k}>n^{3}-75\chi(b)\eta r.

(127)

Note that the condition (
127
) cannot hold for
k=l_{0}
because
\sigma_{l_{0}}\leq n^{2}/2<n^{3}-75\chi(b)\eta r
, based on the assumption that
(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})^{2}\geq 32(K+7)\log(N+n)+64(\log 9)r
. Therefore,
i_{0}
must satisfy
i_{0}\leq l_{0}-1
.

We shall fix such an index
i_{0}
throughout the rest of the proof. The goal is to demonstrate that the following holds with a probability of at least
1-10(N+N)^{-K}
: assume any
i_{0}\leq k\leq s\leq r_{0}
that fulfills
\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}\geq 75\chi(b)\eta r
. For any
j\in\llbracket k,s\rrbracket
, there exists
j_{0}\in\llbracket k,s\rrbracket
such that
\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}\in S_{\sigma_{j_{0}}}
and (
41
) is satisfied.

Before moving forward with the proof, we review several results and introduce necessary notations collected from
[
64
]
. The proofs of these results are identical to these in
[
64
]
, utilizing Lemma
26
, and we will not repeat them here.

Lemma 37

(Eigenvalue location criterion, Lemma 21 from
[
64
]
)
.

Assume
\mathcal{A}
has rank
2r
with the spectral decomposition
\mathcal{A}=\mathcal{U}\mathcal{D}\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}
, where
\mathcal{U}
is an
(N+n)\times 2r
matrix satisfying
\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\mathcal{U}=I_{2r}
and
\mathcal{D}
is a
2r\times 2r
diagonal matrix with non-zero
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{2r}
on the diagonal. Then the eigenvalues of
\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{E}
outside of
[-\|\mathcal{E}\||,\|\mathcal{E}\|]
are the zeros of the function

z\mapsto\det(\mathcal{D}^{-1}-\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}G(z)\mathcal{U}).

Moreover, the algebraic multiplicity of each eigenvalue matches the corresponding multiplicity of each zero.

Define the functions

f(z):=\det(\mathcal{D}^{-1}-\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}G(z)\mathcal{U}),\qquad g(z):=\det\left(\mathcal{D}^{-1}-\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)\mathcal{U}\right),

where
\Phi(z)
is given in (
30
). Observe that, by Lemma
26
,
1/\phi_{1}(z)
,
1/\phi_{2}(z)
and thus
\Phi(z)
are well-defined for any
|z|>M
. Therefore,
f
and
g
are both complex analytic in the region
\{z\in\mathbb{C}:|z|>M\}
. Furthermore, a direct computation using (
34
) suggests that the zeros of
g(z)
are the values
z\in\mathbb{C}
which satisfy the equations
\phi_{1}(z)\phi_{2}(z)=\sigma_{l}^{2}.

Recall from (
39
) and (
32
) that

\varphi(z)=\phi_{1}(z)\phi_{2}(z)=(z-\tr\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{d}}G(z))(z-\tr\mathcal{I}^{\mathrm{u}}G(z)).

We use the function

\xi(b)=1+\frac{1}{2(b-1)^{2}}.

(128)

The subsequent lemma establishes a set of properties exhibited by
\varphi
within the complex plane as well as on the real axis.

Lemma 38

(Lemma 22 from
[
64
]
)
.

The function
\varphi
satisfies the following properties.

(i)

For
z,w\in\mathbb{C}
with
|z|,|w|,|z+w|\geq M
,

\left(1-\frac{1}{2(b-1)^{2}}\right)|z^{2}-w^{2}|\leq|\varphi(z)-\varphi(w)|\leq\xi(b)|z^{2}-w^{2}|.

(129)

(ii)

(Monotone)
\varphi
is real-valued and strictly increasing on
[M,\infty)
.

(iii)

(Crude bounds)
0<\varphi(z)<z^{2}
for any
z\in[M,\infty)
.

Fix an index
j\in\llbracket 1,r_{0}\rrbracket
. Since
\varphi(M)<M^{2}
and
\lim_{z\to\infty}\varphi(z)=\infty
, it follows from the previous lemma that there exists a unique positive real number
z_{j}>M
such that
\varphi(z_{j})=\sigma_{j}^{2}
. Similarly, if
\sigma_{l}>M
for
\sigma_{l}\neq\sigma_{j}
, then there exists a unique positive real number
z_{l}
with
\varphi(z_{l})=\sigma_{l}^{2}
so that
z_{j}>z_{l}
if
l>j
and
z_{j}<z_{l}
if
l<j
.

For the next result, we define the half space

H_{j}:=\{z\in\mathbb{C}:\operatorname{Re}(z)\geq z_{j}-20\chi(b)\eta r\}\quad\text{with }\chi(b)=1+\frac{1}{4b(b-1)}.

Proposition 39

(Proposition 23 from
[
64
]
)
.

Under the assumptions of Theorem
5
, for every
z\in H_{j}
,

|z|\geq\sigma_{j}\geq M.

In particular,

\displaystyle\sigma_{j}\leq z_{j}\leq\chi(b)\sigma_{j}.

(130)

Proposition 40

(Proposition 24 from
[
64
]
)
.

If
\sigma_{j}>\frac{1}{2}n^{2}
, then
|z_{j}-\sigma_{j}|\leq\frac{3b}{b-1}\frac{1}{n}
.

We now complete the proof of Theorem
29
. Let
j
be a fixed index in
\llbracket i_{0},r_{0}\rrbracket
. We will work in the set
H_{j}\cap S_{\sigma_{j}}
, where
S_{\sigma_{j}}
is specified in (
40
). It follows from Corollary 2.14 in
[
48
]
that

\frac{|f(z)-g(z)|}{|g(z)|}\leq\left(1+\varepsilon(z)\right)^{2r}-1,

(131)

where

\varepsilon(z):=\left\|\left(\mathcal{D}^{-1}-\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)\mathcal{U}\right)^{-1}\right\|\left\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}(G(z)-\Phi(z))\mathcal{U}\right\|.

The next result facilitates the comparison of the numbers of zeros of
f
and
g
inside a region and will be used repeatedly in the later arguments.

Lemma 41
.

For any region
\mathcal{K}\subset\mathbb{C}
with closed contour
\partial\mathcal{K}
, if
\varepsilon(z)\leq\frac{0.34}{r}
for all
z\in\partial\mathcal{K}
, then the number of zeros of
f
inside
\mathcal{K}
is the same as the number of zeros of
g
inside
\mathcal{K}
.

Proof.

Continuing from (
131
), we find that

\frac{|f(z)-g(z)|}{|g(z)|}\leq\left(1+\frac{0.34}{r}\right)^{2r}-1\leq e^{0.68}-1<1

(132)

for each
z\in\partial\mathcal{K}
. Therefore, by Rouché’s theorem, we conclude that the numbers of zeros of
f
and
g
inside
\mathcal{K}
are the same.
∎

In the remaining of the proof, we work on the event

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}:=\left\{\max_{z\in\mathsf{D}}|z|^{2}\left\|\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\left(G(z)-\Phi(z)\right)\mathcal{U}\right\|\leq\eta\right\}\cap\left\{\max_{l\in\llbracket 1,r_{0}\rrbracket:\sigma_{l}>\frac{1}{2}n^{2}}|\widetilde{\sigma}_{l}-\sigma_{l}|\leq\eta r\right\},

(133)

where
\mathsf{D}=\{z\in\mathbb{C}:2b(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})\leq|z|\leq 2n^{3}\}.

By Lemma
28
and Lemma
31
, the event
\mathcal{F}

holds with probability at least
1-9(N+n)^{-K}-(N+n)^{-1.5r^{2}(K+4)}>1-10(N+n)^{-K}.

We first bound
\varepsilon(z)
for
z\in\mathsf{D}
. By Proposition 11 from
[
64
]
,

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\mathcal{D}^{-1}-\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)\mathcal{U}\right)^{-1}\right\|=\max_{1\leq l\leq r}\frac{\sigma_{l}}{|\sigma_{l}^{2}-\phi_{1}\phi_{2}|}\mathcal{Q}^{1/2},

where

\mathcal{Q}:=|\phi_{1}\phi_{2}|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{l}^{2}(|\phi_{1}|^{2}+|\phi_{2}|^{2})+\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{l}\left[4|\phi_{1}\phi_{2}|^{2}|\phi_{1}+\bar{\phi}_{2}|^{2}+\sigma_{l}^{2}(|\phi_{1}|^{2}-|\phi_{2}|^{2})^{2}\right]^{1/2}.

Recall
\chi(b)=1+\frac{1}{4b(b-1)}.
Using (
36
) from Lemma
26
, for
z\in\mathsf{D}
, we get
|\phi_{i}(z)|\leq\chi(b)|z|
for
i=1,2
, and

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}

\displaystyle\leq\chi(b)^{4}|z|^{4}+\chi(b)^{2}\sigma_{l}^{2}|z|^{2}+\chi(b)^{2}\sigma_{l}|z|^{2}\sqrt{\sigma_{l}^{2}+4\chi(b)^{2}|z|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\chi(b)^{4}|z|^{4}+\chi(b)^{2}\sigma_{l}^{2}|z|^{2}+\chi(b)^{2}\sigma_{l}|z|^{2}(\sigma_{l}+2\chi(b)|z|)

\displaystyle\leq\chi(b)^{4}|z|^{4}+2\chi(b)^{2}\sigma_{l}^{2}|z|^{2}+2\chi(b)^{3}\sigma_{l}|z|^{3}

\displaystyle\leq\chi(b)^{2}|z|^{2}\left(\chi(b)|z|+\sqrt{2}\sigma_{l}\right)^{2},

and thus

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\mathcal{D}^{-1}-\mathcal{U}^{\mathrm{T}}\Phi(z)\mathcal{U}\right)^{-1}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\chi(b)|z|\max_{1\leq l\leq r}\frac{\sigma_{l}(\chi(b)|z|+\sqrt{2}\sigma_{l})}{|\sigma_{l}^{2}-\varphi(z)|}.

Hence, we obtain that on the event
\mathcal{F}
,

\displaystyle\varepsilon(z)\leq\max_{1\leq l\leq r}\chi(b)\frac{\eta}{|z|}\frac{\sigma_{l}(\chi(b)|z|+\sqrt{2}\sigma_{l})}{|\sigma_{l}^{2}-\varphi(z)|}

(134)

for all
z\in\mathsf{D}
. Note that
S_{\sigma_{j}}\subset\mathsf{D}
for all
j\in\llbracket i_{0},r_{0}\rrbracket
.

For each
j\in\llbracket i_{0},r_{0}\rrbracket
, we take
\mathcal{C}_{j}
to be the circle of radius
20\chi(b)\eta r
centered at
z_{j}
and contained in
H_{j}\cap S_{\sigma_{j}}
. Note that
\mathcal{C}_{j}
’s may intersect each other. For any
i_{0}\leq k\leq s\leq r_{0}
satisfying
\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}\geq 75\chi(b)\eta r
. Let

\mathcal{K}_{k,s}:=\cup_{l=k}^{s}\mathcal{C}_{l}.

We now restrict ourselves to values of
z
contained on
\partial\mathcal{K}_{k,s}
. The goal is to show
\varepsilon(z)
is small for all
z\in\mathcal{\partial}\mathcal{K}_{k,s}
. Continuing from (
134
), it suffices to show

\displaystyle\chi(b)\frac{\eta}{|z|}\frac{\sigma_{l}(\chi(b)|z|+\sqrt{2}\sigma_{l})}{|\sigma_{l}^{2}-\varphi(z)|}

(135)

is small for all
1\leq l\leq r.

Fix
z\in\mathcal{\partial}\mathcal{K}_{k,s}
. Assume
z\in\mathcal{C}_{j_{0}}
for some
j_{0}\in\llbracket k,s\rrbracket
. Then

|z-z_{j_{0}}|=20\chi(b)\eta r.

Note that
\sigma_{j_{0}}\geq 80b\eta r
. Using (
130
), we have

\displaystyle|z|\leq z_{j_{0}}+20\chi(b)\eta r\leq\chi(b)\left(1+\frac{1}{4b}\right)\sigma_{j_{0}},

\displaystyle|z|\geq z_{j_{0}}-20\chi(b)\eta r\geq\left(1-\frac{\chi(b)}{4b}\right)\sigma_{j_{0}}.

(136)

We split the discussion into two cases:
|\sigma_{l}-\sigma_{j_{0}}|\leq 120\eta r
and
|\sigma_{l}-\sigma_{j_{0}}|>120\eta r
.

Case 1.
For any
l\in\llbracket 1,r\rrbracket
satisfying
|\sigma_{l}-\sigma_{j_{0}}|\leq 120\eta r
, observe that
|z-z_{l}|\geq 20\chi(b)\eta r
. In view of (
129
), , we have

\displaystyle|\sigma_{l}^{2}-\varphi(z)|

\displaystyle=|\varphi(z_{l})-\varphi(z)|\geq\left(1-\frac{1}{2(b-1)^{2}}\right)|z_{l}^{2}-z^{2}|\geq\frac{1}{2}|z_{l}-z||z_{l}+z|

\displaystyle\geq 10\chi(b)\eta r|z_{l}+z|\geq\left(1-\frac{\chi(b)}{4b}\right)\sigma_{j_{0}}+\sigma_{l},

where, in the last inequality, we used

\displaystyle|z_{l}+z|=|z_{l}+z_{j_{0}}+z-z_{j_{0}}|

\displaystyle\geq z_{l}+z_{j_{0}}-20\chi(b)\eta r

\displaystyle\geq\sigma_{l}+\sigma_{j_{0}}-20\chi(b)\eta r\geq\left(1-\frac{\chi(b)}{4b}\right)\sigma_{j_{0}}+\sigma_{l}

by (
130
) and the supposition
\eta r\leq{\sigma_{j_{0}}}/{80b}
.
Combining with (
D
), we estimate (
135
) as follows:

\displaystyle\chi(b)\frac{\eta}{|z|}\frac{\sigma_{l}(\chi(b)|z|+\sqrt{2}\sigma_{l})}{|\sigma_{l}^{2}-\varphi(z)|}\leq\frac{1}{r}\frac{1}{10(1-\frac{\chi(b)}{4b})}\frac{\sigma_{l}}{\sigma_{j_{0}}}\frac{\chi(b)^{2}(1+\frac{1}{4b})\sigma_{j_{0}}+\sqrt{2}\sigma_{l}}{(1-\frac{\chi(b)}{4b})\sigma_{j_{0}}+\sigma_{l}}.

Note that
\sigma_{l}\leq\sigma_{j_{0}}+120\eta r\leq(1+120/80b)\sigma_{j_{0}}\leq(7/4)\sigma_{j_{0}}
for
b\geq 2
. Also,
\chi(b)\leq\chi(2)=9/8
for
b\geq 2
. We further obtain that

\displaystyle\chi(b)\frac{\eta}{|z|}\frac{\sigma_{l}(\chi(b)|z|+\sqrt{2}\sigma_{l})}{|\sigma_{l}^{2}-\varphi(z)|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{r}\frac{1}{10(1-9/64)}\frac{7}{4}\frac{(9/8)^{3}\sigma_{j_{0}}+\sqrt{2}\sigma_{l}}{(1-9/64)\sigma_{j_{0}}+\sigma_{l}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{r}\frac{7}{40(1-9/64)}\frac{(9/8)^{3}}{1-9/64}<\frac{0.34}{r}.

Case 2. For any
l\in\llbracket 1,r\rrbracket
satisfying
|\sigma_{l}-\sigma_{j_{0}}|>120\eta r
,
we start with

\displaystyle|\sigma_{l}^{2}-\varphi(z)|

\displaystyle\geq|\sigma_{l}^{2}-\sigma_{j_{0}}^{2}|-|\sigma_{j_{0}}^{2}-\varphi(z)|=|\sigma_{l}^{2}-\sigma_{j_{0}}^{2}|-|\varphi(z_{j_{0}})-\varphi(z)|

\displaystyle\geq|\sigma_{l}-\sigma_{j_{0}}|(\sigma_{l}+\sigma_{j_{0}})-20\chi(b)\xi(b)\eta r|z_{j_{0}}+z|.

by (
129
) and
|z-z_{j_{0}}|=20\chi(b)\eta r
. Since

\displaystyle|z_{j_{0}}+z|

\displaystyle\leq 2z_{j_{0}}+|z-z_{j_{0}}|=2z_{j_{0}}+20\chi(b)\eta r

\displaystyle\leq 2\chi(b)\sigma_{j_{0}}+20\chi(b)\eta r\leq 2\chi(b)\left(1+{1}/{8b}\right)\sigma_{j_{0}}\leq\frac{34}{16}\chi(b)\sigma_{j_{0}}

and
|\sigma_{l}-\sigma_{j_{0}}|>120\eta r
, we further get

\displaystyle|\sigma_{l}^{2}-\varphi(z)|

\displaystyle\geq|\sigma_{l}-\sigma_{j_{0}}|(\sigma_{l}+\sigma_{j_{0}})-20\chi(b)^{2}\xi(b)\frac{34}{16}\frac{1}{120}|\sigma_{l}-\sigma_{j_{0}}|(\sigma_{l}+\sigma_{j_{0}})

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{17}{48}\chi(b)^{2}\xi(b)\right)|\sigma_{l}-\sigma_{j_{0}}|(\sigma_{l}+\sigma_{j_{0}}).

Hence, using (
D
), we get

\displaystyle\chi(b)\frac{\eta}{|z|}\frac{\sigma_{l}(\chi(b)|z|+\sqrt{2}\sigma_{l})}{|\sigma_{l}^{2}-\varphi(z)|}\leq\frac{\chi(b)\eta}{(1-\frac{\chi(b)}{4b})(1-\frac{17}{48}\chi(b)^{2}\xi(b))}\frac{\sigma_{l}}{\sigma_{j_{0}}}\frac{\chi(b)^{2}(1+\frac{1}{4b})\sigma_{j_{0}}+\sqrt{2}\sigma_{l}}{|\sigma_{l}-\sigma_{j_{0}}|(\sigma_{l}+\sigma_{j_{0}})}.

To continue the estimates, we simply use the fact that
\chi(b),\xi(b)
are decreasing. Thus
\chi(b)\leq\chi(2)=9/8
and
\xi(b)=1+1/(2(b-1)^{2})\leq 3/2
for
b\geq 2
. Hence,

\displaystyle\chi(b)\frac{\eta}{|z|}\frac{\sigma_{l}(\chi(b)|z|+\sqrt{2}\sigma_{l})}{|\sigma_{l}^{2}-\varphi(z)|}\leq\frac{(9/8)^{4}}{\frac{55}{64}(1-\frac{17}{48}(\frac{9}{8})^{2}\frac{3}{2})}\frac{\sigma_{l}}{\sigma_{j_{0}}}\frac{\eta}{|\sigma_{j_{0}}-\sigma_{l}|}<6\frac{\sigma_{l}}{\sigma_{j_{0}}}\frac{\eta}{|\sigma_{j_{0}}-\sigma_{l}|}.

If
\sigma_{l}\leq 2\sigma_{j_{0}}
, then

\displaystyle 6\frac{\sigma_{l}}{\sigma_{j_{0}}}\frac{\eta}{|\sigma_{j_{0}}-\sigma_{l}|}\leq 12\frac{\eta}{120\eta r}=\frac{0.1}{r}.

If
\sigma_{l}\geq 2\sigma_{j_{0}}
, then
\sigma_{l}-\sigma_{j_{0}}\geq 0.5\sigma_{l}
and

\displaystyle 6\frac{\sigma_{l}}{\sigma_{j_{0}}}\frac{\eta}{|\sigma_{j_{0}}-\sigma_{l}|}\leq 12\frac{\eta}{\sigma_{j_{0}}}\leq 12\frac{\eta}{160\eta r}=\frac{0.075}{r}.

Thus, we conclude that

\varepsilon(z)\leq\frac{0.34}{r}

(137)

for all
z\in\mathcal{K}_{k,s}
. By Lemma
41
, the number of zeros of
f
inside
\mathcal{K}_{k,s}
is the same as the number of zeros of
g
inside
\mathcal{K}_{k,s}
.

Since
\min\{\delta_{i_{0}-1},\delta_{r_{0}}\}\geq 75\chi(b)\eta r
by our supposition, we could take
k=i_{0}
and
s=r_{0}
and thus
\mathcal{K}_{i_{0},r_{0}}=\cup_{l=i_{0}}^{r_{0}}\mathcal{C}_{l}
.
Since
g
has
r_{0}-i_{0}+1
zeros inside
\mathcal{K}_{i_{0},r_{0}}
, it follows that
\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{E}
has exactly
r_{0}-i_{0}+1
eigenvalues inside
\mathcal{K}_{i_{0},r_{0}}
.
More generally, for any
i_{0}\leq k\leq s\leq r_{0}
satisfying
\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}\geq 75\chi(b)\eta r
, we conclude that the number of eigenvalues of
\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{E}
inside
\mathcal{K}_{k,s}
is
s-k+1
, the same as the number of zeros of
g
inside
\mathcal{K}_{k,s}
.
It remains to show that these eigenvalues are exactly
\widetilde{\sigma}_{k},\cdots,\widetilde{\sigma}_{s}
. If this is the case, then for any
j\in\llbracket k,s\rrbracket
, there exists
j_{0}\in\llbracket k,s\rrbracket
such that
\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}\in\mathcal{C}_{j_{0}}
and thus

|\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}-z_{j_{0}}|\leq 20\chi(b)\eta r.

In particular,
\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}\in S_{\sigma_{j_{0}}}
. By
\varphi(z_{j_{0}})=\sigma_{j_{0}}^{2}
, (
129
) and (
130
),

\displaystyle|\varphi(\widetilde{\sigma}_{j})-\sigma_{j_{0}}^{2}|=|\varphi(\widetilde{\sigma}_{j})-\varphi(z_{j_{0}})|\leq 20\xi(b)\chi(b)\eta r\left(\widetilde{\sigma}_{j}+\chi(b)\sigma_{j_{0}}\right).

This will complete the proof.

It remains to prove that for any
i_{0}\leq k\leq s\leq r_{0}
satisfying
\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}\geq 75\chi(b)\eta r
, the eigenvalues of
\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{E}
inside
\cup_{l=k}^{s}\mathcal{C}_{l}
are exactly
\widetilde{\sigma}_{k},\cdots,\widetilde{\sigma}_{s}
. We will do so by proving the following claims hold on the event
\mathcal{F}
(see Figure
1
for an illustration):

Figure 1.
Distinct circles
\mathcal{C}_{j}
with centers
z_{j}
on the real line for
i_{0}\leq j\leq r_{0}
.

Claim 1.
For any
i_{0}\leq k\leq s\leq r_{0}
satisfying
\min\{\delta_{k-1},\delta_{s}\}\geq 75\chi(b)\eta r
,
\cup_{l=k}^{s}\mathcal{C}_{l}
does not intersect other circles.

Claim 2.

\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{E}
has exactly
i_{0}-1
eigenvalues larger than
z_{i_{0}}+20\chi(b)\eta r
.

Claim 3.
No eigenvalues of
\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{E}
lie between disjoint circles.

For the moment, let us assume these claims are true. Note that
\widetilde{\sigma}_{i_{0}}
has to lie inside one of the
\mathcal{C}_{j}
’s (
i_{0}\leq j\leq r_{0}
) because it is the largest eigenvalue of
\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{E}
that is no larger than
z_{i_{0}}+20\chi(b)\eta r
(due to
Claim 2
) and thus it satisfies
\widetilde{\sigma}_{i_{0}}>z_{r_{0}}-20\chi(b)\eta r
. Since the number of zeros of
g(z)
located inside
\mathcal{K}_{i_{0},r_{0}}=\cup_{j=i_{0}}^{r_{0}}\mathcal{C}_{j}
, which is
r_{0}-i_{0}+1
, is the same as that of
f(z)
inside
\mathcal{K}_{i_{0},r_{0}}
, we have
\widetilde{\sigma}_{i_{0}},\ldots,\widetilde{\sigma}_{r_{0}}
lie inside
\mathcal{K}_{i_{0},r_{0}}
. The conclusion follows by
Claim 1
,
Claim 3
and the fact that the number of zeros of
g(z)
in each
\mathcal{K}_{k,s}
is the same as that of
f(z)
.

We start with the proof of the
Claim 1
. It suffices to show that if
|\sigma_{l}-\sigma_{j}|\geq 75\chi(b)\eta r
, then
\mathcal{C}_{l}
and
\mathcal{C}_{j}
do not intersect. By Lemma
38
,

|z_{l}^{2}-z_{j}^{2}|\geq\frac{1}{\xi(b)}|\varphi(z_{l})-\varphi(z_{j})|=\frac{|\sigma_{l}^{2}-\sigma_{j}^{2}|}{\xi(b)}\geq\frac{75\chi(b)\eta r}{\xi(b)}(\sigma_{l}+\sigma_{j}).

Since
|z_{l}^{2}-z_{j}^{2}|=(z_{l}+z_{j})|z_{l}-z_{j}|\leq\chi(b)(\sigma_{l}+\sigma_{j})|z_{l}-z_{j}|
by Proposition
39
, we have

|z_{l}-z_{j}|\geq\frac{75}{\xi(b)}\eta r,

(138)

and thus

\dist(\mathcal{C}_{j},\mathcal{C}_{l})\geq|z_{l}-z_{j}|-40\chi(b)\eta r\geq\frac{75}{\xi(b)}\eta r-40\chi(b)\eta r\geq\left(\frac{75}{\xi(2)}-40\chi(2)\right)\eta r>0.

Next, we prove
Claim 2
. We split the proof into two cases:
i_{0}=1
and
i_{0}>1
.

Case 1:
i_{0}=1
.
We prove that no eigenvalues of
\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{E}
are larger than
z_{1}+20\chi(b)\eta r
. We now take
\mathcal{C}_{0}
to be any circle with radius
20\chi(b)\eta r
centered at a point
z_{0}>z_{1}+20\chi(b)\eta r
on the real line inside the region
H_{1}\cap\hat{S}_{\sigma_{1}}
such that
\dist(z_{1},\mathcal{C}_{0})\geq 20\chi(b)\eta r
. Here

\displaystyle\hat{S}_{\sigma_{1}}:=\{w\in\mathbb{C}:

\displaystyle|\operatorname{Im}(w)|\leq 20\chi(b)\eta r,

\displaystyle 2b(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})+138\eta r\leq\operatorname{Re}(w)\leq\frac{3}{2}\sigma_{1}+20\chi(b)\eta r\}

(139)

is a slight modification of the set
{S}_{\sigma}
in (
40
). Note that
\widetilde{\sigma}_{1}\in\hat{S}_{\sigma_{1}}
: the upper bound
\widetilde{\sigma}_{1}\leq\frac{3}{2}\sigma_{1}
follows from the Weyl’s inequality and the supposition
\|\mathcal{E}\|\leq\frac{1}{b}\sigma_{1}\leq\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{1}
; the lower bound is because it is the largest eigenvalue and
\widetilde{\sigma}_{1}\geq z_{j}-20\chi(b)\eta r\geq\sigma_{j}-20\chi(b)\eta r
for any
j\in\llbracket i_{0},r_{0}\rrbracket
due to fact that the number of eigenvalues of
\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{E}
inside
\cup_{l=i_{0}}^{r_{0}}\mathcal{C}_{l}
is
r_{0}-i_{0}+1
. For
z\in\hat{S}_{\sigma_{1}}
,

2b(\sqrt{N}+\sqrt{n})\leq|z|\leq 40\chi(b)\eta r+\frac{3}{2}\sigma_{2}\leq\frac{40\chi(b)}{80b}\sigma_{1}+\frac{3}{2}\sigma_{1}\leq\frac{57}{32}\sigma_{1}<2n^{2},

hence
z\in\mathsf{D}
and the conclusion of Lemma
28
holds. In particular, the bound (
134
) also holds for
z\in\hat{S}_{\sigma_{1}}
.
We show

\varepsilon(z)<\frac{1}{3r}

for all
z\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
. The proof is similar to the proof of (
137
) and we sketch it here. For any
z\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
, from
|z-z_{0}|=20\chi(b)\eta r
and
z_{0}-z_{1}>40\chi(b)\eta r
, we obtain

|z|\leq z_{0}+20\chi(b)\eta r
and

|z|\geq z_{0}-20\chi(b)\eta r\geq z_{1}+20\chi(b)\eta r>\sigma_{1}+20\chi(b)\eta r>\sigma_{1}.

Again, by Lemma
38
, we see for any
1\leq l\leq r
,

\displaystyle|\sigma_{l}^{2}-\varphi(z)|=|\varphi(z_{l})-\varphi(z)|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}|z_{l}^{2}-z^{2}|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}(z_{l}+\operatorname{Re}(z))(\operatorname{Re}(z)-z_{l})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}(\sigma_{l}+z_{0}-20\chi(b)\eta r)(z_{0}-z_{l}-20\chi(b)\eta r)

\displaystyle\geq 10\chi(b)\eta r(\sigma_{l}+z_{0}-20\chi(b)\eta r).

(140)

Plugging these estimates back into (
134
), we see

\displaystyle\varepsilon(z)\leq\max_{1\leq l\leq r}\chi(b)\frac{\eta\sigma_{l}}{\sigma_{1}}\frac{\chi(b)(z_{0}+20\chi(b)\eta r)+\sqrt{2}\sigma_{l}}{10\chi(b)\eta r(z_{0}+\sigma_{l}-20\chi(b)\eta r)}<\frac{1}{5r},

where we used the bound
\chi(b)(z_{0}+20\chi(b)\eta r)+\sqrt{2}\sigma_{l}\leq 2(z_{0}+\sigma_{l}-20\chi(b)\eta r)
in the last inequality.

By Lemma
41
,
f
has the same number of zeros inside
\mathcal{C}_{0}
as
g
. As
g
has no zeros inside
\mathcal{C}_{0}
1
1

1

This follows from Lemma
38
and the fact that
\operatorname{Im}(\varphi(z))\neq 0
whenever
\operatorname{Im}z\neq 0
for all
|z|>M
.
,
\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{E}
has no eigenvalues inside
\mathcal{C}_{0}
. Since the circle
\mathcal{C}_{0}
was arbitrarily chosen inside this region, we conclude that
\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{E}
has no eigenvalues larger than
z_{1}+20\chi(b)\eta r
.

Case 2:
i_{0}>1
.

On the event
\mathcal{F}
, we have

\max_{l\in\llbracket 1,r_{0}\rrbracket;\sigma_{l}>n^{2}/2}|\widetilde{\sigma}_{l}-\sigma_{l}|\leq\eta r.

(141)

Note that
\sigma_{i_{0}-1}>n^{3}>n^{2}
. Combining (
141
), Proposition
40
and

z_{i_{0}-1}-z_{i_{0}}\geq\frac{75}{\xi(b)}\eta r\geq\frac{75}{\xi(2)}\eta r=50\eta r,

which follows from the supposition
\delta_{i_{0}-1}\geq 75\chi(b)\eta r
and the same argument as (
138
), we get

\displaystyle\widetilde{\sigma}_{i_{0}-1}\geq\sigma_{i_{0}-1}-\eta r\geq z_{i_{0}-1}-\frac{3b}{b-1}\frac{1}{n}-\eta r\geq z_{i_{0}}+50\eta r-\frac{6}{n}>z_{i_{0}}+20\chi(b)\eta r.

Hence,
\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{E}
has at least
i_{0}-1
eigenvalues larger than
z_{i_{0}}+20\chi(b)\eta r
.

We first consider
\sigma_{i_{0}}>\frac{1}{2}n^{2}
. It follows from (
141
) and Proposition
40
that

\widetilde{\sigma}_{i_{0}}\leq\sigma_{i_{0}}+\eta r\leq z_{i_{0}}+\frac{3b}{b-1}\frac{1}{n}+\eta r\leq z_{i_{0}}+\frac{6}{n}+\eta r<z_{i_{0}}+20\chi(b)\eta r.

This shows that
\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{E}
has exactly
i_{0}-1
eigenvalues larger than
z_{i_{0}}+20\chi(b)\eta r
.

Now consider
\sigma_{i_{0}}\leq\frac{1}{2}n^{2}
. By Weyl’s inequality,
\widetilde{\sigma}_{i_{0}}\leq\sigma_{i_{0}}+\|E\|\leq(1+\frac{1}{b})\sigma_{i_{0}}
. If
(1+\frac{1}{b})\sigma_{i_{0}}\leq z_{i_{0}}+20\chi(b)\eta r
, the proof is already done. Now we assume
(1+\frac{1}{b})\sigma_{i_{0}}>z_{i_{0}}+20\chi(b)\eta r
. If
(1+\frac{1}{b})\sigma_{i_{0}}-(z_{i_{0}}+20\chi(b)\eta r)<20\eta r
, following (
130
), we have
\chi(b)\sigma_{i_{0}}\geq z_{i_{0}}>(1+\frac{1}{b})\sigma_{i_{0}}-20(\chi(b)+1)\eta r
and thus
\sigma_{i_{0}}<80b\eta r\frac{(b-1)(\chi(b)+1)}{4b-5}
. Note that
\frac{(b-1)(\chi(b)+1)}{4b-5}
is decreasing for
b\geq 2
and
\frac{(b-1)(\chi(b)+1)}{4b-5}\leq 17/24
. Hence,
\sigma_{i_{0}}<80b\eta r
contradicts the supposition that
\sigma_{i_{0}}\geq 80b\eta r
.

It suffices to assume
(1+\frac{1}{b})\sigma_{i_{0}}-(z_{i_{0}}+20\chi(b)\eta r)\geq 20\eta r
, which implies that
z_{i_{0}}\leq(1+\frac{1}{b})\sigma_{i_{0}}-20(\chi(b)+1)\eta r
. To prove
\widetilde{\sigma}_{i_{0}}\leq z_{i_{0}}+20\chi(b)\eta r
, we show that
f
has no zeros on the interval
(z_{i_{0}}+20\chi(b)\eta r,(1+\frac{1}{b})\sigma_{i_{0}})
. The proof is similar to the proof of
Case 1
when
i_{0}=1
. We only mention the differences. Define
\hat{S}_{\sigma_{i_{0}}}
as in (
D
) and the bound (
134
) also holds for
z\in\hat{S}_{\sigma_{i_{0}}}
. The goal is to show
\varepsilon(z)<1/3r
for all
z\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
, where
\mathcal{C}_{0}
is any circle with radius
10\eta r
centered at a point
z_{0}\in(z_{i_{0}}+20\chi(b)\eta r,(1+\frac{1}{b})\sigma_{i_{0}})
inside the region
H_{i_{0}}\cap\hat{S}_{\sigma_{i_{0}}}
such that
\dist(z_{0},z_{i_{0}}+20\chi(b)\eta r)\geq 10\eta r
and
\dist(z_{0},(1+\frac{1}{b})\sigma_{i_{0}})\geq 10\eta r
. If so, by Lemma
41
,
f
has the same number of zeros inside
\mathcal{C}_{0}
as
g
. Note that
g
has no zeros inside
\mathcal{C}_{0}
since
\operatorname{Im}(\varphi(z))\neq 0
whenever
\operatorname{Im}z\neq 0
for all
|z|>M
and
z_{i_{0}-1}\geq\sigma_{i_{0}-1}-\frac{3b}{b-1}\frac{1}{n}>n^{2}-\frac{3b}{b-1}\frac{1}{n}>\frac{3}{2}\sigma_{i_{0}}\geq(1+\frac{1}{b})\sigma_{i_{0}}
by Proposition
40
. Since
\mathcal{C}_{0}
was arbitrarily chosen,
\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{E}
has no eigenvalues on
(z_{i_{0}}+20\chi(b)\eta r,(1+\frac{1}{b})\sigma_{i_{0}})
.

It remains to bound
\varepsilon(z)
from (
134
). Note that
z_{0}-z_{i_{0}}\geq 10\eta r+20\chi(b)\eta r
and
(1+\frac{1}{b})\sigma_{i_{0}}-z_{0}\geq 10\eta r
. For
z\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
, from
|z-z_{0}|=10\eta r
, we get
|z|\geq z_{0}-10\eta r\geq z_{i_{0}}+20\chi(b)\eta r\geq\sigma_{i_{0}}+20\chi(b)\eta r>\sigma_{i_{0}}
and
|z|\leq z_{0}+10\eta r\leq(1+\frac{1}{b})\sigma_{i_{0}}.

The same arguments as those in
Case 1
yield that

\max_{i_{0}\leq l\leq r}\chi(b)\frac{\eta}{|z|}\frac{\sigma_{l}(\chi(b)|z|+\sqrt{2}\sigma_{l})}{|\sigma_{l}^{2}-\varphi(z)|}<\frac{1}{3r}

for any
z\in\mathcal{C}_{0}
.
We only need to control

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq i_{0}-1}\chi(b)\frac{\eta}{|z|}\frac{\sigma_{l}(\chi(b)|z|+\sqrt{2}\sigma_{l})}{|\sigma_{l}^{2}-\varphi(z)|}.

For any
1\leq l\leq i_{0}-1
, using similar computation from (
D
), we get

\displaystyle|\sigma_{l}^{2}-\varphi(z)|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}(\sigma_{l}+z_{0}-10\eta r)(z_{l}-z_{0}-10\eta r).

Plugging in
z_{0}\geq z_{i_{0}}+10\eta r+20\chi(b)\eta r\geq\sigma_{i_{0}}+10\eta r+20\chi(b)\eta r
, we obtain

\sigma_{l}+z_{0}-20\chi(b)\eta r\geq\sigma_{l}+\sigma_{i_{0}}+20\chi(b)\eta r.

From
\sigma_{l}\geq n^{3}
, we see
\sigma_{i_{0}}\leq\frac{1}{2}n^{2}<\frac{1}{2}\sigma_{l}
. This, together with (
130
) and
z_{0}\leq(1+\frac{1}{b})\sigma_{i_{0}}-10\eta r
, implies that

\displaystyle z_{l}-z_{0}-10\eta r

\displaystyle\geq\sigma_{l}-(1+\frac{1}{b})\sigma_{i_{0}}\geq\sigma_{l}-\frac{1}{2}(1+\frac{1}{b})\sigma_{l}\geq\frac{1}{4}\sigma_{l}.

Hence,
|\sigma_{l}^{2}-\varphi(z)|\geq\frac{1}{8}\sigma_{l}(\sigma_{l}+\sigma_{i_{0}}+20\chi(b)\eta r)
and

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq l\leq i_{0}-1}\chi(b)\frac{\eta}{|z|}\frac{\sigma_{l}(\chi(b)|z|+\sqrt{2}\sigma_{l})}{|\sigma_{l}^{2}-\varphi(z)|}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq l\leq i_{0}-1}\chi(b)\frac{\eta\sigma_{l}}{\sigma_{i_{0}}}\frac{\chi(b)(1+\frac{1}{b})\sigma_{i_{0}}+\sqrt{2}\sigma_{l}}{\frac{1}{8}(\sigma_{l}+\sigma_{i_{0}}+20\chi(b)\eta r)\sigma_{l}}

\displaystyle<45\frac{\eta}{\sigma_{i_{0}}}\leq\frac{45\eta}{160\eta r}<\frac{1}{3r}

using the assumption
\sigma_{i_{0}}\geq 80b\eta r\geq 160\eta r
and the bound
\chi(b)\leq\chi(2)=9/8
.
Therefore,
\varepsilon(z)<{1}/{3r}
for all
z\in\mathcal{C}_{0}.

The proof of
Claim 3
is similar to the previous argument. Let
\mathcal{C}_{j_{1}},\mathcal{C}_{j_{2}}
be two disjoint circles for
j_{1},j_{2}\in\llbracket i_{0},r_{0}\rrbracket
. Then
|z_{j_{1}}-z_{j_{2}}|>40\chi(b)\eta r
. Let
\mathsf{d}:=\dist(\mathcal{C}_{j_{1}},\mathcal{C}_{j_{2}})>0.
We show that
\mathcal{A}+\mathcal{E}
has no eigenvalues lying on the real line between
\mathcal{C}_{j_{1}}
and
\mathcal{C}_{j_{2}}
. Take any point
x
on the real line between the two circles so that
\mathcal{C}_{x}
, the circle centered at
x
with radius
\mathsf{r}:=\frac{1}{10}\min\{d,20\chi(b)\eta r\}
(say), is inside the region
H_{j_{1}}\cap S_{\sigma_{j_{1}}}
or
H_{j_{2}}\cap S_{\sigma_{j_{2}}}
, where
\dist(x,\mathcal{C}_{j_{1}})>\mathsf{r}
and
\dist(x,\mathcal{C}_{j_{2}})>\mathsf{r}
. Then using similar calculations as in the proof of
Claim 2
, it suffices to show that
\varepsilon(z)<{1}/{3r}
. The remaining arguments are similar to those in the proof of
Claim 2
; we omit the details.
